# Sāṃkhya — Complete Learning Session

> **Catalogue identity:** Philosophy Optional · Philosophy Paper I — Indian Philosophy · Syllabus item 16.
> **Official syllabus wording (verbatim, printed spelling retained):** `Samkhya; Prakrit; Purusa; Causation; Liberation.`
> **Terminology rule:** the English concept leads; the accurate Sanskrit term in IAST follows immediately in brackets.
> **Evidence discipline:** doctrine, textual attribution and previous-year wording follow the repository's canonical owners. Model answers are independent learner practice and are never presented as official keys.

<a id="formal_session-002-basic-learning-session-94d9ce5ec8"></a>
## BASIC LEARNING SESSION

<a id="formal_session-003-how-this-basic-block-is-organised-067eb55e32"></a>
### How this Basic block is organised

The fifteen sessions below run in teaching order, not in source order. Each session opens with a
definition, gives a gateway idea in plain language, shows the concept before it argues it, then
supplies the named evidence, the answer-grabbing line, the must-write keywords, a worked
answer-paragraph model, the exam route, the traps and a rapid revision strip.

Everything needed for a complete 10-, 15- or 20-mark answer on any routed question is inside this
Basic block. The Optional Advanced block near the end of this document is enrichment; no core
answer depends on it.

| Session | What it settles | Why it comes here |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Threefold suffering (**duḥkha-traya**) and the promise of final cessation | The system is built to answer a practical problem, so the problem comes first |
| 2 | System identity: name, texts, enumeration, non-theism | Fixes what "Sāṃkhya" refers to before doctrine is argued |
| 3 | Three means of valid knowledge (**pramāṇa**) and the inferential burden | Nothing later can be proved until the instruments of proof are fixed |
| 4 | Cognition: the inner instrument (**antaḥkaraṇa**) and reflection | Explains how an unconscious mind can seem conscious |
| 5 | Primordial material nature (**prakṛti**): what it is and why it is needed | Introduces the first ultimate |
| 6 | The five proofs of primordial material nature | Discharges the burden created in Session 3 |
| 7 | The three qualities (**guṇa**) and their modification | Supplies the internal mechanism of all evolution |
| 8 | Conscious witness (**puruṣa**): nature and five proofs | Introduces the second ultimate |
| 9 | Plurality of witnesses, purposive nature, and empirical self versus witness | Completes the account of selfhood |
| 10 | The twenty-five principles (**tattva**) in exact order | The full cosmological–psychological sequence |
| 11 | Subtle body (**liṅga-śarīra**), transmigration and karma | Explains continuity without making the witness move |
| 12 | Pre-existence of the effect (**satkāryavāda**): the five arguments | The causal thesis |
| 13 | Real transformation (**pariṇāmavāda**) against rival causal theories | Positions Sāṃkhya among competitors |
| 14 | Bondage, discriminative knowledge, isolation (**kaivalya**) and non-theism | The soteriology the system was built for |
| 15 | The contact problem, Śaṃkara's critique, inter-school placement and verdict | The evaluative close every high-mark answer needs |

<a id="formal_session-004-session-1-why-sāṃkhya-exists-threefold-suffering-and-the-demand-for-fina-d84693fd10"></a>
### SESSION 1 — WHY SĀṂKHYA EXISTS: THREEFOLD SUFFERING AND THE DEMAND FOR FINAL CESSATION

<a id="formal_session-005-definition-what-this-is-called-b6547bd82e"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Sāṃkhya begins from a practical complaint, not from curiosity: living
beings are struck by suffering from three directions, ordinary remedies only postpone it, and the
system claims that one particular kind of knowledge ends it permanently.

**Technical definition.** The threefold suffering (**duḥkha-traya**) is the classification of
affliction into the internal or psychophysical (**ādhyātmika**), the affliction caused by other
beings and the external natural order (**ādhibhautika**), and the affliction attributed to cosmic,
elemental or unseen agencies (**ādhidaivika**). Sāṃkhya holds that visible and ritual means give
incomplete or temporary relief, and that discriminative knowledge alone yields final,
non-returning cessation.

<a id="formal_session-006-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-c5004989ad"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Think of a person with a recurring illness. Painkillers work, but only for a while. A ritual cure
may comfort, but the disease returns. Sāṃkhya says the same thing about existence: every remedy
that operates *inside* the field of nature is itself made of nature, so it can only rearrange
suffering, never abolish it. To end suffering you must step outside the field — and that step is a
piece of knowledge, not an action.

That single move explains the whole architecture. If liberation is by knowledge, the system must
say exactly *what* is to be known. It answers: know that you are the witness and not the field.
Everything else in Sāṃkhya — the two ultimates, the qualities, the twenty-five principles, the
theory of causation — exists to make that one discriminative statement true and intelligible.

<a id="formal_session-007-visual-the-three-arrows-of-suffering-and-the-one-exit-1da2bf0e04"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE THREE ARROWS OF SUFFERING AND THE ONE EXIT

```text
                 ┌───────────────────────────────────────────┐
                 │        THE BEING WHO SUFFERS              │
                 │   body + senses + mind + intellect        │
                 └───────────────────────────────────────────┘
                    ▲                ▲                ▲
                    │                │                │
   ADHYATMIKA ──────┘                │                └────── ADHIDAIVIKA
   (ādhyātmika)                      │                        (ādhidaivika)
   FROM WITHIN                       │                        FROM UNSEEN /
   disease of body,                  │                        COSMIC-ELEMENTAL
   affliction of mind          ADHIBHAUTIKA                   agencies in the
                               (ādhibhautika)                 traditional list
                               FROM OTHER BEINGS
                               humans, animals,
                               material surroundings

   ORDINARY EXITS               WHY THEY FAIL
   ─────────────────────────    ─────────────────────────────────────────────
   medicine, wealth, comfort →  relief is partial and it decays
   ritual and sacrificial means → result is produced, therefore perishable
                                 and repeatable, therefore not final

   THE SĀṂKHYA EXIT
   ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   discriminative knowledge (viveka-jñāna): "I am the witness; I am not the
   field" → false attribution stops → nature stops performing for that witness
   → isolation (kaivalya): final, non-returning, not repeatable
```

*What the diagram shows: three independent sources of affliction converge on one psychophysical
complex; only an exit that is not itself a product of that complex can be final.*

<a id="formal_session-008-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-493c98839d"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Suffering is universal and threefold.** The classification is not decorative. It is designed
   to be exhaustive: whatever afflicts you comes from your own psychophysical constitution, from
   other beings and the surrounding material order, or from agencies you cannot control or
   observe. If the list is exhaustive, no remedy that addresses only one arrow can be complete.
2. **Visible remedies are structurally partial.** Medicine, protection and prosperity operate
   within the field of nature. They redistribute conditions; they do not remove the possibility of
   affliction.
3. **Ritual remedies are structurally impermanent.** Anything *produced* is liable to destruction.
   A result won by a sacrificial act is a produced result, so it is exhaustible and must be won
   again. Sāṃkhya therefore treats scripturally promised results as belonging to the same category
   as visible ones: better, but not final.
4. **Therefore the required remedy must be non-productive.** It cannot manufacture a new state; it
   must correct a mistake. A mistake is corrected by knowledge.
5. **Hence the whole system is enumerative.** "Sāṃkhya" is traditionally connected both with
   discriminative or right knowledge and with enumeration — fitting a system that promises
   liberation through the knowledge of twenty-five principles. The list of principles is the
   content of the liberating knowledge.

> **Qualification to keep.** The threefold diagnosis is a doctrinal framing rather than a separately
> printed syllabus noun. It earns its place because it explains why liberation, not cosmology, is
> the organising end of the entire system — and because it is what makes the enumeration of
> principles a soteriological exercise rather than a taxonomy.

<a id="formal_session-009-named-evidence-you-can-cite-4cbea617fc"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **Īśvarakṛṣṇa, *Sāṃkhyakārikā*** — the earliest extant systematic classical text of the school,
  and the reference point for the threefold suffering, the proofs, the evolutionary sequence and
  the account of liberation.
- **The threefold classification itself** — internal (**ādhyātmika**), from other beings and the
  environment (**ādhibhautika**), and from cosmic or unseen agencies (**ādhidaivika**).
- **The exhaustibility argument against ritual means** — a produced result is perishable, therefore
  repeatable, therefore not final.

<a id="formal_session-010-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-27d311cf1f"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Sāṃkhya is not primarily a cosmology that happens to end in liberation; it is a soteriology that
> is forced to become a cosmology, because a system which promises release by knowledge must first
> specify, exhaustively, what there is to be known.

<a id="formal_session-011-must-write-keywords-e97b1d6d75"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **threefold suffering (duḥkha-traya)**
- **internal affliction (ādhyātmika)**
- **affliction from other beings and surroundings (ādhibhautika)**
- **affliction from cosmic or unseen agencies (ādhidaivika)**
- **final and non-returning cessation (ātyantika)**
- **discriminative knowledge (viveka-jñāna)**
- **enumeration of twenty-five principles**

<a id="formal_session-012-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-6d6dd5cb07"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Sāṃkhya's metaphysical apparatus is generated by a therapeutic demand, not by
speculative interest.

**Named evidence.** The system opens from the impact of threefold suffering — internal
(**ādhyātmika**), from other beings and the material environment (**ādhibhautika**), and from
cosmic or unseen agencies (**ādhidaivika**) — and rejects both visible remedies and ritual means as
partial or exhaustible.

**Analysis.** Because a produced result is by definition perishable, no *action* can supply a final
cure; only a correction of misidentification can, and a correction of misidentification is
knowledge. This is precisely why the school must enumerate principles: the discriminative knowledge
that liberates is knowledge that the witness is not any of the twenty-four evolutes.

**Qualification.** The point is not that Sāṃkhya despises ordinary or ritual remedies; it grades
them. They are effective within nature and ineffective against the structural condition that makes
affliction recur, which is why the school treats them as preliminary rather than false.

<a id="formal_session-013-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-ae1d910b84"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

This session is not itself a printed question, but it supplies the opening move for every
liberation-facing routed question — most directly **2018 Q5(e)** on the plurality of witnesses,
where "whose bondage and whose release?" is the decisive pressure, and **2023 Q5(b)** on Śaṃkara's
criticism, where the practical stake of the dualism must be visible before the relation problem is
raised.

<a id="formal_session-014-upsc-traps-540b8aaf2c"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** present the threefold suffering as a printed syllabus term; present it as the
  doctrinal starting point that explains why the syllabus clause ends with "Liberation".
- Do **not** say Sāṃkhya rejects Vedic means as false. It grades them as exhaustible.
- Do **not** import Buddhist "first noble truth" language. The classifications differ, and the
  Sāṃkhya remedy is discriminative knowledge of two ultimates, not the eightfold discipline.

<a id="formal_session-015-rapid-revision-d2d71fff3f"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Three arrows: internal · from beings and surroundings · from unseen or cosmic agencies.
- Visible remedies are partial; ritual results are produced and therefore exhaustible.
- A final cure must correct a mistake, not manufacture a state → knowledge, not action.
- Knowledge must have content → enumeration of twenty-five principles.
- One line: *the system enumerates because it promises release by discrimination.*

<a id="formal_session-016-session-2-system-identity-name-texts-scope-and-the-non-theistic-frame-46d1d110e1"></a>
### SESSION 2 — SYSTEM IDENTITY: NAME, TEXTS, SCOPE AND THE NON-THEISTIC FRAME

<a id="formal_session-017-definition-what-this-is-called-8135424969"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** "Classical Sāṃkhya" means one specific, argued position — the
dualism of one unconscious primordial nature and many inactive conscious witnesses, with real
evolution and no creator — as set out in the earliest surviving systematic text of the school.

**Technical definition.** Classical Sāṃkhya is the system of Īśvarakṛṣṇa's *Sāṃkhyakārikā*: a
realist dualism of primordial material nature (**prakṛti**) and conscious witness (**puruṣa**),
committed to the pre-existence of the effect in its material cause (**satkāryavāda**) in the form
of real transformation (**pariṇāmavāda**), to a plurality of witnesses, and to a non-theistic
(**nirīśvara**) account of world, bondage and release.

<a id="formal_session-018-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-315f6d1afe"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

There is a trap hidden in the word "Sāṃkhya". Sāṃkhya-like ideas appear in medical, epic and
Upaniṣadic contexts, and later authors rewrite the school in a theistic direction. If you quote all
of these as one thing, your answer becomes indefensible. So fix the reference first: when the
examiner writes "Sāṃkhya", the default is the *Sāṃkhyakārikā* system. Anything earlier is a
precursor and anything later is a development, and both must be labelled as such.

<a id="formal_session-019-visual-the-textual-ladder-and-what-each-rung-licences-4a5a700476"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE TEXTUAL LADDER AND WHAT EACH RUNG LICENCES

```text
  TIME →

  ┌────────────────────┐  Kapila → Āsuri → Pañcaśikha
  │  TRADITIONAL       │  Traditional founders. Foundational works are NOT
  │  ATTRIBUTION       │  extant in securely recoverable form.
  └────────────────────┘  LICENCE: name them; do not quote them.
             │
             ▼
  ┌────────────────────┐  ĪŚVARAKṚṢṆA, SĀṂKHYAKĀRIKĀ
  │  CLASSICAL         │  Earliest extant systematic classical text.
  │  BASELINE   ★      │  LICENCE: this is "Sāṃkhya" unless told otherwise.
  └────────────────────┘
             │
             ├────────────► Gauḍapāda's commentary
             ├────────────► Vācaspati Miśra, TATTVAKAUMUDĪ
             │              LICENCE: major explanatory traditions on the Kārikā.
             ▼
  ┌────────────────────┐  SĀṂKHYAPRAVACANA-SŪTRA + Vijñānabhikṣu
  │  LATER PHASE       │  Later textual phase, often more theistic and
  │  ⚠ CAUTION         │  reconciliatory toward Vedānta.
  └────────────────────┘  LICENCE: cite as a later reading; NEVER project it
                          back into every claim of the classical system.
```

*What the diagram shows: four rungs with four different permissions. Most errors in this topic are
permission errors — using a later theistic reading to describe the classical non-theistic system.*

<a id="formal_session-020-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-4a6b4e51e0"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Traditional lineage, honest limits.** Tradition associates the school with Kapila, followed by
   Āsuri and Pañcaśikha. Their foundational works are not extant in securely recoverable form, so
   they are names in a lineage rather than quotable authorities.
2. **The classical baseline.** The earliest extant systematic classical text is Īśvarakṛṣṇa's
   *Sāṃkhyakārikā*, with major explanatory traditions including Gauḍapāda's commentary and
   Vācaspati Miśra's *Tattvakaumudī*.
3. **The later phase must be labelled.** The *Sāṃkhyapravacana-sūtra* and Vijñānabhikṣu's
   commentary represent a later textual and often more theistic phase. They must not be projected
   uncritically back into every claim of the classical Kārikā system.
4. **What the name signals.** "Sāṃkhya" is traditionally connected both with discriminative or
   right knowledge and with enumeration. Both senses fit a system that seeks liberation through
   knowledge of twenty-five principles.
5. **Non-theism, stated precisely.** In the classical system no God is required for either bondage
   or liberation. The safest formulation is that the position is *non-theistic* or *God-unproved*
   rather than a demonstration that no divine being could exist: primordial material nature and
   conscious witnesses are held sufficient for the explanatory work.
6. **The boundary with Yoga.** Sāṃkhya supplies the dualist metaphysics of primordial nature and
   many witnesses that classical Yoga substantially shares. Yoga then develops a distinct practical
   psychology — mind-field, afflictions, meditative absorption, the eight-limbed discipline, and a
   special conscious witness identified as God. Therefore Yoga is a *bounded comparison* in a
   Sāṃkhya answer, never a source of Sāṃkhya doctrine.

<a id="formal_session-021-named-evidence-you-can-cite-5425e0bbe8"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

| Item | Exact status | Answer use |
|---|---|---|
| Kapila, Āsuri, Pañcaśikha | Traditional founders; foundational works not securely recoverable | Name the lineage; do not attribute arguments |
| Īśvarakṛṣṇa, *Sāṃkhyakārikā* | Earliest extant systematic classical text | The controlling reference for every claim |
| Gauḍapāda's commentary | Major explanatory tradition on the Kārikā | Supporting commentarial authority |
| Vācaspati Miśra, *Tattvakaumudī* | Major commentary | Supporting commentarial authority |
| *Sāṃkhyapravacana-sūtra*; Vijñānabhikṣu | Later, often more theistic phase | Cite only as a labelled later development |

<a id="formal_session-022-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-b70be0233a"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The first discipline in writing on Sāṃkhya is chronological hygiene: the school's classical
> identity is fixed by the *Sāṃkhyakārikā*, so a later theistic commentary cannot be used to soften
> the non-theism that the classical system deliberately argues for.

<a id="formal_session-023-must-write-keywords-d9b89f4298"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **Īśvarakṛṣṇa's *Sāṃkhyakārikā***
- **Vācaspati Miśra's *Tattvakaumudī***
- **later theistic phase (Vijñānabhikṣu)**
- **non-theistic (nirīśvara)**
- **enumeration and discriminative knowledge**
- **chief principle (pradhāna)**
- **bounded comparison with Yoga**

<a id="formal_session-024-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-af3ad4f281"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Precision about which Sāṃkhya is being discussed is itself a philosophical commitment,
not a bibliographic courtesy.

**Named evidence.** Classical Sāṃkhya is fixed by Īśvarakṛṣṇa's *Sāṃkhyakārikā* and its commentarial
tradition, including Vācaspati Miśra's *Tattvakaumudī*, while the *Sāṃkhyapravacana-sūtra* and
Vijñānabhikṣu represent a later and often more theistic phase.

**Analysis.** The difference is doctrinally decisive: the classical system argues that primordial
nature and plural witnesses are jointly sufficient to explain world, bondage and release, so
importing a later God-affirming reading does not merely add a thesis — it removes the explanatory
economy that the classical arguments were built to establish.

**Qualification.** The later phase is not thereby worthless; it is a legitimate development that
shows the pressure Vedāntic theism exerted on the school. It simply cannot be used as evidence
about what Īśvarakṛṣṇa's system held.

<a id="formal_session-025-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-c6cf48a37d"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

Directly load-bearing for **2025 Q7(a)** on why Śaṃkara treats Sāṃkhya as his chief opponent
(**pradhāna malla**): the answer only works if "Sāṃkhya" means the classical non-theistic system
with an unconscious first principle. It also protects **2024 Q7(a)**, which explicitly asks for the
evolution "as propounded in *Sāṃkhyakārikā*".

<a id="formal_session-026-upsc-traps-9edec8871e"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** call classical Sāṃkhya "atheistic" without qualification; write **non-theistic** or
  **God-unproved**, and then give the arguments.
- Do **not** use Vijñānabhikṣu's theism as evidence about the Kārikā system.
- Do **not** treat "unorthodox" as a synonym for non-theistic; Sāṃkhya is an orthodox
  (**āstika**) school that nevertheless does not require a creator.
- Do **not** blur Sāṃkhya and Yoga. Yoga owns the mind-field, afflictions, eight limbs, meditative
  absorption and God as a special witness.

<a id="formal_session-027-rapid-revision-1274a53e62"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Four rungs: traditional founders → *Sāṃkhyakārikā* ★ → commentaries → later theistic phase.
- "Sāṃkhya" = enumeration + discriminative knowledge; both senses fit twenty-five principles.
- Classical position: non-theistic, not anti-theistic-by-proof.
- Orthodox school, no creator. Yoga is a bounded comparison, not a source.

<a id="formal_session-028-session-3-three-means-of-valid-knowledge-and-the-inferential-burden-b862d05286"></a>
### SESSION 3 — THREE MEANS OF VALID KNOWLEDGE AND THE INFERENTIAL BURDEN

<a id="formal_session-029-definition-what-this-is-called-2ee02a447c"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Sāṃkhya allows only three ways of knowing anything. That decision
creates a problem it must then solve, because the two things the system most needs — primordial
nature and the witness — cannot be seen.

**Technical definition.** Sāṃkhya admits exactly three means of valid knowledge (**pramāṇa**):
perception (**dṛṣṭa / pratyakṣa**), inference (**anumāna**) and reliable verbal testimony
(**āptavacana / śabda**). Other candidates — comparison (**upamāna**), postulation
(**arthāpatti**), non-apprehension (**anupalabdhi**), inclusion or probability (**sambhava**) and
traditional report (**aitihya**) — are not dismissed as useless but reduced to, or included within,
these three.

<a id="formal_session-030-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-92ec15d7c4"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Imagine a court that accepts only three kinds of evidence: what the witness saw, what can be
validly inferred, and the testimony of a reliable authority. Now the court must decide a case about
something nobody can ever see. It cannot invent a fourth kind of evidence without abandoning its
own rule. So it must show that inference alone — of a particular, carefully specified kind — is
strong enough to reach the invisible defendant.

That is exactly Sāṃkhya's situation, and it is why the proofs of primordial nature and of the
witness are not rhetorical flourishes. They are the discharge of a debt the school incurred when it
restricted the instruments of knowledge to three.

<a id="formal_session-031-visual-the-three-instruments-the-reductions-and-the-burden-b49e161d03"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE THREE INSTRUMENTS, THE REDUCTIONS, AND THE BURDEN

```text
  ADMITTED (exactly three)                    REDUCED (not rejected as useless)
  ─────────────────────────────────────       ────────────────────────────────────────
  1 PERCEPTION (pratyakṣa)                    upamāna  → inference / testimony
    ascertainment with respect to a           arthāpatti → inference
    presented object through the senses       anupalabdhi → perception's failure-conditions
    in contact with it                        sambhava → inference
                                              aitihya  → testimony when reliable,
  2 INFERENCE (anumāna) — THREE KINDS                     otherwise rejected
    ├ pūrvavat        cause/antecedent → effect        e.g. clouds → rain
    ├ śeṣavat         effect/residue  → cause          e.g. one drop → the sea is salty
    └ sāmānyato-dṛṣṭa general correlation, connection  e.g. sun in different positions
                      NOT itself perceived                  → the sun moves

  3 RELIABLE TESTIMONY (āptavacana / śabda)
    word of a non-defective source, pre-eminently Veda/śruti, where the object is
    not otherwise easily known

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ THE BURDEN THIS CREATES                                                  │
  │ root nature (mūla-prakṛti) and witness (puruṣa) are BEYOND THE SENSES    │
  │ (atīndriya) → perception cannot reach them                               │
  │ testimony alone would beg the question against non-Vedic opponents       │
  │ ⇒ THE WHOLE WEIGHT FALLS ON SĀMĀNYATO-DṚṢṬA INFERENCE                    │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

*What the diagram shows: the left column is what Sāṃkhya keeps, the right column is what it absorbs,
and the box is the debt that the five proofs of nature and the five proofs of the witness are built
to repay.*

<a id="formal_session-032-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-3fdb2b42b7"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

**The three definitions, precisely.**

- **Perception** is the ascertainment with respect to a presented object (**prativiṣayādhyavasāya**)
  of objects through the sense-capacities in contact with them.
- **Inference** is threefold, as tabulated above.
- **Reliable testimony** is the word of a trustworthy source, pre-eminently Veda or **śruti**, where
  the speaker or text is not defective and the object is not otherwise easily known.

**Why the reductions matter.** Sāṃkhya is not being economical for its own sake. Each reduction is
an argued claim:

| Candidate | Reduced to | Reason in one line |
|---|---|---|
| comparison (**upamāna**) | inference / testimony | Similarity works because a prior verbal report is applied inferentially to a present case. |
| postulation (**arthāpatti**) | inference | Postulation is inference to the best explanatory condition; no separate instrument is needed. |
| non-apprehension (**anupalabdhi**) | perception's failure-conditions | Non-perception means the conditions for perceiving are absent or the object is unavailable. |
| inclusion / probability (**sambhava**) | inference | Moving from a larger unit to a smaller or probable implication is inferential dependence. |
| traditional report (**aitihya**) | testimony when reliable | Mere tradition has force only when it becomes trustworthy verbal cognition. |

**Why non-perception does not refute.** Sāṃkhya lists the standard causes of non-perception:
excessive distance, excessive proximity, defect of the organ, inattention of mind, subtlety
(**saukṣmya**), obstruction by an intervening object, being overpowered by something else, and
admixture with like things. Root nature's imperceptibility is assigned to **subtlety**; the witness
is likewise beyond the senses rather than non-existent. This is the single most important defensive
move in the topic: *imperceptibility is a fact about the knower's instruments, not a proof of
absence.*

**Why general-correlation inference is decisive.** Ordinary inference of the first two kinds needs
the invariable relation (**vyāpti**) to be established by observing both terms together. That is
impossible for a term never observed at all. General-correlation inference (**sāmānyato-dṛṣṭa**) can
reach such a term because it does not require the two terms to be seen together; it works through a
general correlation of *kinds*. Since perception cannot reach what is beyond the senses, and
testimony alone would beg the question against non-Vedic opponents, the argumentative burden falls
here.

**Objections and replies.**

- **Cārvāka objection.** The invariable relation cannot be secured for a term never perceived;
  inference to root nature or the witness is therefore speculative.
  **Reply.** Strict perceptualism cannot explain ordinary causal reasoning either; manifest,
  dependent, purposive and objectifiable experience demands an unmanifest material ground and a
  conscious witness.
- **Nyāya objection.** General-correlation inference is legitimate, but Sāṃkhya overuses it. A
  plurality of atoms plus an intelligent God explains the ordered world more economically than an
  unobservable material first cause.
  **Reply.** The continuity between cause and effect, the qualitative character of experience and
  the determinacy of production are better explained by a single material nature; adding God is
  unnecessary because it does not explain the material continuity itself.

> **Presupposition to state openly.** Sāṃkhya presupposes that inference from general correlation
> can legitimately reach an entity of a kind never itself observed as a sense-object. If that
> presupposition is denied, the five proofs of primordial nature become analogical guesses rather
> than philosophical inferences.

<a id="formal_session-033-named-evidence-you-can-cite-72bbeb15bd"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **Three-instrument scheme** with the five named reductions.
- **The threefold division of inference**, with the sun's changing positions as the standard
  example of general-correlation inference.
- **The eight causes of non-perception**, with subtlety assigned to root nature.
- **The named objectors**: Cārvāka on the invariable relation; Nyāya on economy and an intelligent
  maker.

<a id="formal_session-034-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-43ae1b4eb5"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Sāṃkhya's epistemology is not a preface to its metaphysics but a constraint upon it: by admitting
> only three instruments of knowledge, the school obliges itself to reach its two ultimates by
> general-correlation inference alone, and every later proof is the payment of that self-imposed
> debt.

<a id="formal_session-035-must-write-keywords-0a2c0bc26c"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **perception (pratyakṣa) as prativiṣayādhyavasāya**
- **inference (anumāna): pūrvavat · śeṣavat · sāmānyato-dṛṣṭa**
- **reliable testimony (āptavacana / śabda)**
- **beyond the senses (atīndriya)**
- **subtlety (saukṣmya)**
- **invariable relation (vyāpti)**
- **reduction of upamāna, arthāpatti, anupalabdhi, sambhava, aitihya**

<a id="formal_session-036-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-d76d149fc2"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Sāṃkhya's restriction to three instruments of knowledge is what makes its metaphysical
proofs necessary rather than ornamental.

**Named evidence.** The school admits only perception, inference and reliable testimony, reducing
comparison, postulation, non-apprehension, inclusion and traditional report to these three; and it
classes root nature's imperceptibility under subtlety (**saukṣmya**) in the standard list of eight
causes of non-perception.

**Analysis.** Because both ultimates are beyond the senses and testimony would be question-begging
against a non-Vedic opponent, the entire evidential weight falls on general-correlation inference
(**sāmānyato-dṛṣṭa**), which alone can reach a term whose invariable relation was never observed
directly. The five proofs of nature and the five proofs of the witness are therefore the exact shape
this epistemology forces.

**Qualification.** The move is powerful but not cost-free: it rests on the presupposition that a
general correlation of kinds can license an inference to an entity of a kind never observed, and it
is precisely this presupposition that Cārvāka denies and Nyāya accepts while accusing Sāṃkhya of
over-extending it.

<a id="formal_session-037-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-852487dd7d"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

Load-bearing for **2019 Q8(c)** — "which proof actually shows that there can be only one nature?" —
because the answer must first explain *why* nature has to be proved at all. It also strengthens
**2022 Q5(a)** on the proofs for the witness: the examiner's word "evaluate" is best met by showing
that the proofs share one inferential form.

<a id="formal_session-038-upsc-traps-7c6745c8a6"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** write that Sāṃkhya *rejects* comparison or postulation. It **reduces** them.
- Do **not** say root nature is imperceptible because it does not exist; the school assigns its
  imperceptibility to **subtlety** within a standard eight-item list.
- Do **not** attribute six instruments (an Advaita count) or four (the classical Nyāya count) to
  Sāṃkhya.
- Do **not** describe general-correlation inference as "vague inference". It is a technical kind
  defined by an unperceived connection between the terms.

<a id="formal_session-039-rapid-revision-32e2e3139f"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Three instruments: perception · inference · reliable testimony.
- Five reductions: comparison, postulation, non-apprehension, inclusion, traditional report.
- Inference is threefold; general-correlation inference carries the metaphysics.
- Eight causes of non-perception; root nature falls under subtlety.
- One line: *three instruments in, two invisible ultimates to prove, therefore inference does all
  the work.*

<a id="formal_session-040-session-4-cognition-the-inner-instrument-reflection-and-apparent-agency-fb93d102cc"></a>
### SESSION 4 — COGNITION: THE INNER INSTRUMENT, REFLECTION AND APPARENT AGENCY

<a id="formal_session-041-definition-what-this-is-called-e20f2a503d"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** In Sāṃkhya the mind is not conscious and consciousness does not
think. Knowing happens when an insentient mental organ takes the shape of an object and the
witness's light falls on it — so the event *looks* like a conscious mind knowing, although neither
of its two components is that.

**Technical definition.** The inner instrument (**antaḥkaraṇa**) has three functions — determinative
intellect (**buddhi**), the I-maker (**ahaṃkāra**) and the coordinating mind (**manas**). With the
five cognitive capacities and five action capacities it forms the thirteenfold instrument
(**trayodaśa-karaṇa**). Intellect alone makes the final ascertainment (**adhyavasāya**). Being
predominantly luminous (**sattva**-predominant), intellect receives the reflection of the witness
(**puruṣa-cchāyā-āpatti / buddhi-pratibimba**), and this reflection generates false appropriation
(**abhimāna**) — "I am the doer, knower, enjoyer" — which is the psychological root of bondage.

<a id="formal_session-042-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-61982c98ef"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Think of a clean mirror in a dark room and a lamp outside the doorway. The mirror cannot glow; the
lamp cannot take shapes. But put them in the right relation and you get something that looks
exactly like a glowing image. Sāṃkhya says empirical knowing is that: the intellect takes the shape
of the object, the witness supplies the light, and the resulting cognition seems to be a conscious
mind's own act.

Two mistakes then become natural. The insentient organ appears conscious, and the inactive witness
appears to be an agent. Both mistakes are one mistake seen from two ends — and undoing it is what
liberation means.

<a id="formal_session-043-visual-the-thirteenfold-instrument-and-the-two-way-mistake-2f116d7386"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE THIRTEENFOLD INSTRUMENT AND THE TWO-WAY MISTAKE

```text
   OUTER: THE DOORS (dvāra)                    INNER: THE DOORKEEPER (dvārin)
   ─────────────────────────────────           ──────────────────────────────────────
   5 COGNITIVE CAPACITIES                      MANAS      coordination, attention,
     (jñānendriya / buddhīndriya)                         synthesis of sense-data
     hearing · touch · sight ·                          │
     taste · smell                                      ▼
                                              AHAṂKĀRA   appropriation as "I" and
   5 ACTION CAPACITIES                                   "mine"
     (karmendriya)                                     │
     speech · grasping · locomotion ·                  ▼
     excretion · reproduction                 BUDDHI     FINAL ASCERTAINMENT
                                                         (adhyavasāya) — judgement,
   5 + 5 + 3 = THIRTEENFOLD INSTRUMENT                   discrimination
             (trayodaśa-karaṇa)                          sattva-predominant ⇒ transparent

                       ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐
     PURUṢA  ─────────►│  reflection falls into transparent buddhi   │
     pure light        │  (puruṣa-cchāyā-āpatti / buddhi-pratibimba) │
     never acts        └────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                        │
                       ┌────────────────┴────────────────┐
                       ▼                                 ▼
        THE INSENTIENT (jaḍa) ORGAN          THE INACTIVE WITNESS
        APPEARS CONSCIOUS                    APPEARS TO BE AN AGENT
                       └────────────────┬────────────────┘
                                        ▼
                          FALSE APPROPRIATION (abhimāna)
                       "I am the doer / knower / enjoyer"
                              = the root of bondage
```

*What the diagram shows: a strict division of labour on top, a single reflection event in the
middle, and a symmetrical two-way misattribution at the bottom. The whole of Sāṃkhya soteriology is
the reversal of that bottom row.*

<a id="formal_session-044-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-efd4301521"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **Three inner functions, sharply distinguished.** Intellect determines and ascertains; the
   I-maker appropriates; the mind coordinates. Writing them as three synonyms for "mind" destroys
   the argument.
2. **Doors and doorkeeper.** The external faculties function as doors (**dvāra**); the inner organ
   functions as doorkeeper (**dvārin**) because it receives, coordinates and determines what enters
   cognition. Sense-capacities present; only intellect concludes.
3. **Only intellect ascertains.** Final ascertainment (**adhyavasāya**) belongs to intellect alone.
   This matters for the exam because the 2024 question asks precisely for the difference between
   cosmic intellect, determinative intellect and the I-maker.
4. **Why intellect and not another organ receives the reflection.** Intellect is
   **sattva**-predominant and therefore transparent. Transparency is what makes reflection possible;
   a **tamas**-heavy organ would obstruct rather than receive.
5. **The two-way appearance.** Through conjunction or the semblance of conjunction, the unconscious
   internal instrument appears conscious, while the inactive witness appears to be an agent.
   Productive agency belongs to the qualities and evolutes of nature; witnessing belongs to the
   witness.
6. **From reflection to bondage.** The reflection generates false appropriation (**abhimāna**) — "I
   am the doer/knower/enjoyer" — and that appropriation is the psychological root of bondage. Note
   the exact ordering: reflection is a structural fact, appropriation is the error, bondage is the
   consequence.

> **Answer-level formulation.** For Sāṃkhya the mind does not know by itself and the witness does
> not act by itself; empirical knowing is a reflected-consciousness event located in the intellect.

<a id="formal_session-045-named-evidence-you-can-cite-5640fe2c04"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

| Term | Precise function |
|---|---|
| **buddhi** | final ascertainment (**adhyavasāya**), judgement, discrimination |
| **ahaṃkāra** | appropriation as "I" and "mine" |
| **manas** | coordination, attention, synthesis of sense-data |
| **five cognitive capacities** | access to sound, touch, form, taste and smell |
| **five action capacities** | speech, grasping, locomotion, excretion, reproduction |
| **dvāra / dvārin** | doors versus doorkeeper |
| **buddhi-pratibimba** | reflection of the witness in transparent intellect |
| **abhimāna** | false appropriation; psychological root of bondage |
| **jaḍa** | insentient — the standing description of every evolute |

<a id="formal_session-046-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-65369977e4"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Sāṃkhya's most modern-sounding claim is that cognition and consciousness are different things:
> the intellect processes without being aware, the witness is aware without processing, and what we
> call a conscious thought is the borrowed light of the second falling on the first.

<a id="formal_session-047-must-write-keywords-132a022ca3"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **inner instrument (antaḥkaraṇa)**
- **thirteenfold instrument (trayodaśa-karaṇa)**
- **final ascertainment (adhyavasāya)**
- **doors and doorkeeper (dvāra / dvārin)**
- **reflection in intellect (buddhi-pratibimba)**
- **false appropriation (abhimāna)**
- **insentient (jaḍa)**

<a id="formal_session-048-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-890c30eb9a"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Sāṃkhya explains the appearance of a conscious mind without granting consciousness to
any mental organ.

**Named evidence.** The inner instrument is threefold — intellect, I-maker and mind — and with ten
external capacities forms the thirteenfold instrument; the external faculties are doors and the
inner organ the doorkeeper; and intellect, being **sattva**-predominant and therefore transparent,
receives the reflection of the witness.

**Analysis.** This yields a symmetrical illusion rather than a single one: the insentient organ
appears conscious and the inactive witness appears to be an agent, and the false appropriation "I am
the doer" is generated exactly at the junction. The school therefore has a psychological mechanism
for bondage, not merely a metaphysical label for it.

**Qualification.** The mechanism is explanatory only if reflection into an insentient medium is
itself intelligible; critics reply that a reflection which changes nothing in either relatum cannot
generate a genuine cognitive event, and Sāṃkhya's answer at this point is analogical rather than
demonstrative.

<a id="formal_session-049-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-e2c4300f2a"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

Essential to **2019 Q5(d)** on the metaphysical status of the empirical self and the witness, since
the empirical self just *is* the witness as conditioned through intellect. Essential again to
**2024 Q7(a)**, whose second half asks explicitly for the difference between cosmic intellect,
determinative intellect and the I-maker.

<a id="formal_session-050-upsc-traps-cf080da9fd"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** say the mind (**manas**) determines or judges. It coordinates; intellect determines.
- Do **not** say the witness knows objects directly. The witness illuminates the intellect's
  operations.
- Do **not** describe the reflection as a real modification of the witness; that would make bondage
  real and liberation impossible.
- Do **not** count the thirteenfold instrument as thirteen *tattvas* separate from the
  twenty-five; the instrument is a functional grouping drawn from within the list.

<a id="formal_session-051-rapid-revision-9195f99b8e"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Inner instrument: intellect determines · I-maker appropriates · mind coordinates.
- Thirteenfold instrument = 3 inner + 5 cognitive + 5 action capacities.
- Doors present; the doorkeeper decides.
- Transparent intellect receives the witness's reflection → false appropriation → bondage.
- One line: *knowing is processing plus borrowed light, and mistaking the two for one is bondage.*

<a id="formal_session-052-session-5-primordial-material-nature-what-it-is-and-why-the-system-needs-4d74df2486"></a>
### SESSION 5 — PRIMORDIAL MATERIAL NATURE: WHAT IT IS AND WHY THE SYSTEM NEEDS IT

<a id="formal_session-053-definition-what-this-is-called-713ca67a9f"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Primordial material nature is the one unconscious stuff out of which
everything objective evolves — not only bodies and elements, but also intellect, ego, mind and the
senses. It is never created and never perceived; it is inferred as the root of everything that
changes.

**Technical definition.** Primordial material nature (**prakṛti**) is the unmanifest, unconscious,
primordial material principle from which the entire objective and psychophysical world evolves. It
is called the chief principle (**pradhāna**) because it is the primary material basis of all
empirical manifestation, and the unmanifest (**avyakta**) because in its primordial condition it is
not yet differentiated into manifest principles. It is the ultimate material cause of the world
within Sāṃkhya's dualist ontology.

<a id="formal_session-054-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-b2b14c2e46"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Two words will keep you out of trouble here. First, *material* in Sāṃkhya does not mean "physical".
Intellect, ego, mind and the senses are all products of nature; they are unconscious machinery,
however subtle. Second, *root* does not mean "first event". Nature is not a beginning in time; it
is the standing condition from which manifestation issues whenever equilibrium is disturbed.

So when you write "nature", picture a single reservoir of unconscious potency that includes the
mind, not a heap of matter opposed to the mind. Getting this right prevents the commonest error in
the topic: reading Sāṃkhya as a mind–body dualism instead of a consciousness–nature dualism.

<a id="formal_session-055-visual-what-nature-covers-and-what-it-excludes-73d44052b0"></a>
#### VISUAL — WHAT "NATURE" COVERS AND WHAT IT EXCLUDES

```text
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  PRIMORDIAL MATERIAL NATURE (prakṛti)                                    │
  │  one · unconscious · uncreated · active · constituted by three qualities │
  │                                                                          │
  │  NAMES AND WHAT EACH NAME EMPHASISES                                      │
  │    pradhāna  → it is the CHIEF material principle                         │
  │    avyakta   → it is UNMANIFEST prior to differentiated evolution         │
  │    mūla-prakṛti → it is the ROOT: cause of all, effect of none            │
  │                                                                          │
  │  WHAT IS INSIDE THE RESERVOIR                                             │
  │    ┌─────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────┐  │
  │    │ intellect   │ ego-maker    │ mind + ten   │ subtle elements →    │  │
  │    │ (buddhi)    │ (ahaṃkāra)   │ capacities   │ gross elements       │  │
  │    └─────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────────┘  │
  │    ⇒ "material" here means UNCONSCIOUS, not merely physical               │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                   ▲
                                   │  NOT inside: it is the other ultimate
                                   │
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  CONSCIOUS WITNESS (puruṣa) — many · inactive · changeless · beyond the   │
  │  qualities · never an evolute and never an evolvent                       │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

*What the diagram shows: the dividing line in Sāṃkhya does not run between mind and body — it runs
between everything unconscious, however subtle, and pure consciousness.*

<a id="formal_session-056-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-772a521985"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

**Why a second principle is required at all.**

1. The world is marked by plurality, transformation, dependence, purposiveness and structured
   manifestation.
2. A school that accepts real effects, real bondage and real liberation needs a real material
   source from which empirical diversity emerges. Sāṃkhya is a realist; it cannot dissolve the
   world into appearance.
3. The witness is changeless and inactive, so it cannot itself become the world. A changeless
   principle that turned into the world would not be changeless.
4. Therefore a second principle is required: active but unconscious nature.

**The three names, and what each licenses in an answer.**

| Name | What it emphasises | Answer use |
|---|---|---|
| **prakṛti** | the productive nature of the principle | the general term |
| **pradhāna** | it is the *chief* material principle | Śaṃkara's target term in **pradhāna malla** |
| **avyakta** | it is *unmanifest* before differentiation | contrasts with the manifest (**vyakta**) evolutes |
| **mūla-prakṛti** | it is *root*: cause of all, effect of none | fixes its place in the fourfold classification |

**Exam-safe definition.** Nature is the unmanifest chief principle (**avyakta pradhāna**),
constituted by the three qualities.

**The precise scope of "material".** Nature is not "matter" in the modern physicalist sense. It
includes the roots of intellect, ego, mind, the senses, the subtle elements and the gross elements.
This is why Sāṃkhya can be a dualism and still hold that all mental functioning is unconscious
machinery.

<a id="formal_session-057-named-evidence-you-can-cite-5adf9f0920"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **The four names** — nature, chief principle, unmanifest, root nature — with their distinct
  emphases.
- **The uncreatedness claim** — nature is not itself produced; it is the ultimate material cause
  within the dualist ontology.
- **The scope claim** — intellect, ego, mind, capacities, subtle and gross elements are all
  evolutes of nature.

<a id="formal_session-058-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-1d97f6c05b"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Sāṃkhya's dualism is often misdescribed as mind against matter; in fact it places intellect, ego
> and mind firmly on the side of unconscious nature, so the real fault-line runs between everything
> that changes and the one thing that merely watches.

<a id="formal_session-059-must-write-keywords-c6a27b98e2"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **primordial material nature (prakṛti)**
- **chief principle (pradhāna)**
- **unmanifest (avyakta)** versus **manifest (vyakta)**
- **root nature (mūla-prakṛti)**
- **ultimate material cause**
- **unconscious, not merely physical**

<a id="formal_session-060-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-7c76e4ac8e"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The postulation of primordial material nature is forced by Sāṃkhya's realism, not chosen
for elegance.

**Named evidence.** Nature is defined as the unmanifest chief principle (**avyakta pradhāna**),
uncreated, constituted by the three qualities, and comprehending the roots of intellect, ego, mind,
the capacities, and the subtle and gross elements.

**Analysis.** Because the witness is changeless and inactive, it cannot become the world; and
because Sāṃkhya refuses to treat the world as mere appearance, the transformation has to be located
somewhere real. A single unconscious productive principle is the minimum that satisfies both
constraints, which is why nature is inferred rather than merely assumed.

**Qualification.** The economy is genuine but purchased at a price: once intellect and ego are
placed inside nature, Sāṃkhya must explain how anything unconscious can present an ordered
experience at all — the question that Sessions 4, 9 and 15 return to.

<a id="formal_session-061-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-ce58061fef"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

The definitional spine for **2019 Q8(c)** and **2021 Q6(b)**, and the indispensable opening for
**2024 Q7(a)** on the evolution of nature. It also supplies the term Śaṃkara actually attacks in
**2025 Q7(a)**: the chief principle (**pradhāna**).

<a id="formal_session-062-upsc-traps-1ff6d5a680"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** equate nature with matter as opposed to mind. Mind is *inside* nature.
- Do **not** call nature "created" or "produced". It is the root: cause of all, effect of none.
- Do **not** treat "unmanifest" as a synonym for "non-existent". It means undifferentiated.
- Do **not** substitute Advaita's **māyā** for nature. Nature really transforms; **māyā** produces
  appearance.

<a id="formal_session-063-rapid-revision-155630e112"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- One, unconscious, uncreated, active, constituted by three qualities.
- Four names: nature · chief principle · unmanifest · root nature.
- Contains intellect, ego, mind, capacities, subtle and gross elements.
- Needed because a changeless witness cannot become a changing world.
- One line: *the unmanifest chief principle constituted by the three qualities.*

<a id="formal_session-064-session-6-the-five-proofs-of-primordial-nature-and-the-one-nature-verdic-06ea970146"></a>
### SESSION 6 — THE FIVE PROOFS OF PRIMORDIAL NATURE AND THE ONE-NATURE VERDICT

<a id="formal_session-065-definition-what-this-is-called-f094d41e5f"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Since nature cannot be seen, Sāṃkhya gives five inferences that
together argue there must be one unmanifest material root. One of these five does a job the others
cannot: it shows the root must be *single*.

**Technical definition.** The five standard arguments are: from the finitude and determinateness of
things (**bhedānāṃ parimāṇāt**); from the uniformity running through effects (**samanvayāt**); from
operation due to causal power (**śaktitaḥ pravṛtteś ca**); from the distinction between cause and
effect (**kāraṇa-kārya-vibhāgāt**); and from the undivided source of the diversified universe
(**avibhāgāt vaiśvarūpyasya**). The fifth is the proof that establishes there can be only one
nature.

<a id="formal_session-066-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-29ea731da9"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Do not learn these as five interchangeable slogans. Learn them as five different *jobs*:

- one argues from **limits** (things are finite, so something subtler underlies them);
- one argues from **pattern** (effects hang together, so they share a matrix);
- one argues from **power** (only a potent cause operates, so a competent source exists);
- one argues from **stages** (manifest effect implies unmanifest cause);
- one argues from **unity in diversity** — and only that one delivers *singularity*.

The 2019 examiner asked exactly this: *which* proof shows nature is one? If you have learned five
slogans you will guess. If you have learned five jobs you will answer instantly.

<a id="formal_session-067-visual-five-proofs-five-jobs-one-verdict-411d0bb7c6"></a>
#### VISUAL — FIVE PROOFS, FIVE JOBS, ONE VERDICT

```text
  PROOF                                  WHAT IT ARGUES FROM        WHAT IT DELIVERS
  ─────────────────────────────────────  ─────────────────────────  ─────────────────────
  1 bhedānāṃ parimāṇāt                   finitude, delimitation,    a SUBTLER GROUND
    "from the finitude of things"        measurability             beneath limitation
  ─────────────────────────────────────  ─────────────────────────  ─────────────────────
  2 samanvayāt                           order and common pattern   a COMMON MATERIAL
    "from uniformity in effects"         across diverse products    BASIS
  ─────────────────────────────────────  ─────────────────────────  ─────────────────────
  3 śaktitaḥ pravṛtteś ca                effects arise only through a PRODUCTIVE POWER
    "from operation due to power"        a cause with the relevant  adequate to all
                                         power (śakti)              manifest effects
  ─────────────────────────────────────  ─────────────────────────  ─────────────────────
  4 kāraṇa-kārya-vibhāgāt                cause and effect are       an UNMANIFEST CAUSAL
    "from the cause–effect distinction"  distinguishable stages     MATRIX distinct from
                                                                    the manifest order
  ═════════════════════════════════════  ═════════════════════════  ═════════════════════
  5 avibhāgāt vaiśvarūpyasya         ★   the diversified world is   ⇒ THE ROOT MUST BE
    "from the undivided source of        ONE INTERCONNECTED         ⇒ **ONE**
     the diversified universe"           COSMOS                     ⇒ 2019 Q8(c) ANSWER
  ─────────────────────────────────────  ─────────────────────────  ─────────────────────

  THE BRIDGE THE EXAMINER WANTS AFTER NAMING PROOF 5:
  plurality at the root would defeat the very inference that took us there —
  if the source were many disconnected ultimates, the cosmos would not be
  the ONE interconnected whole from whose undividedness we reasoned back.
```

*What the diagram shows: four proofs establish that there is an unmanifest material root; only the
fifth constrains how many such roots there can be.*

<a id="formal_session-068-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-3e8a42e72c"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

**(1) From the finitude and determinateness of things — bhedānāṃ parimāṇāt.**
Empirical things are many, finite, delimited and measurable. What is finite and determinate points
beyond itself to a more fundamental source from which such determinate forms arise; the manifold
world therefore requires a common antecedent material ground. The force of the argument is not
merely "every effect has a cause", but that *the limited and differentiated presuppose a subtler
ground from which limitation and differentiation emerge*.

**(2) From the uniformity running through effects — samanvayāt.**
Effects display an underlying order and common pattern; diverse products nevertheless reveal
continuity of constitution and dependence. Such systematic coherence suggests a common material
basis. This is an argument from *coordinated* diversity: if effects hang together, they are likely
rooted in one underlying matrix.

**(3) From operation due to causal power — śaktitaḥ pravṛtteś ca.**
Effects arise through the operation of a cause possessing the relevant power (**śakti**). Evolution
is manifestation of what the potent cause can produce; an impotent or unrelated source cannot
generate a determinate effect. Therefore the world-process presupposes an underlying productive
power capable of the full range of manifest effects.

> **Textual control — write this if you can.** The standard reading of *Sāṃkhyakārikā* 15 is
> **śaktitaḥ pravṛtteś ca**, not *kāryataḥ pravṛtteḥ*. The argument is from causal power and
> operation, **not** from conscious purposive planning. Getting this right is what stops the proof
> collapsing into a design argument — which would be fatal for a non-theistic system.

**(4) From the distinction between cause and effect — kāraṇa-kārya-vibhāgāt.**
Cause and effect are distinguishable stages: the unmanifest source and the manifest product are not
identical in appearance. If effects are manifest, multiple and differentiated, their cause must be a
more basic, prior and subtler condition. The point is not total disconnection; cause and effect are
distinguishable without being unrelated.

**(5) From the undivided source of the diversified universe — avibhāgāt vaiśvarūpyasya.**
The world is astonishingly diverse, yet this diversity forms **one interconnected cosmos**. A
diversified whole points back to an undivided source in which later differentiations are not yet
separated. Therefore there must be an original unmanifest, undivided root of the whole
world-process. **This is the proof that shows nature must be ONE**, and it is the strongest
anti-pluralist argument at the level of material causation in the system.

**The 2019 answer, stated exactly.** The proof is **avibhāgāt vaiśvarūpyasya**. Reason: if the
entire articulated universe emerges from an undivided source, that source must itself be singular,
not many independent primal materials. In a ten-marker, do not merely name the proof — add the
logical bridge: *plurality at the root would undermine the very inference to an undivided source of
total cosmic diversity.*

<a id="formal_session-069-named-evidence-you-can-cite-40da34a465"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- All five Sanskrit proof-names, each with its English gloss.
- *Sāṃkhyakārikā* 15 and the correct reading **śaktitaḥ pravṛtteś ca**.
- The explicit verdict that the fifth proof, and only the fifth, delivers singularity.

<a id="formal_session-070-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-371418a108"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Four of Sāṃkhya's proofs establish *that* there is an unmanifest material root; only the argument
> from the undivided source of a diversified universe establishes *how many* there can be, which is
> why it alone answers the question of nature's unity.

<a id="formal_session-071-must-write-keywords-a4df46c665"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **bhedānāṃ parimāṇāt**
- **samanvayāt**
- **śaktitaḥ pravṛtteś ca** (*Sāṃkhyakārikā* 15)
- **kāraṇa-kārya-vibhāgāt**
- **avibhāgāt vaiśvarūpyasya** — the one-nature proof
- **causal power (śakti)**
- **undivided source of a diversified cosmos**

<a id="formal_session-072-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-b8f7ec5948"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Among the five inferences for primordial nature, only the argument from the undivided
source of the diversified universe (**avibhāgāt vaiśvarūpyasya**) proves that nature is one.

**Named evidence.** The first four proofs reason from finitude, from uniformity across effects, from
operation due to causal power as read in *Sāṃkhyakārikā* 15, and from the distinction between
manifest effect and unmanifest cause; the fifth reasons from the fact that cosmic diversity forms a
single interconnected whole.

**Analysis.** The first four are all compatible with a plurality of material roots — several
reservoirs could each be subtler than their products, exhibit pattern, possess power and differ from
their effects. Only the fifth makes unity itself the premise, so that admitting many roots would
destroy the very undividedness from which the inference proceeds.

**Qualification.** The argument is decisive only within Sāṃkhya's own inferential licence: it
assumes that the interconnectedness we observe is genuine and traces back to a single origin rather
than to a coordination among several, which is exactly the assumption a Nyāya atomist declines to
grant.

<a id="formal_session-073-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-3a4bf6afae"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

**2019 Q8(c)** asks this question verbatim — "Which Sāṃkhya proof for the existence of Prakṛti
actually shows that there can be only one Prakṛti? Justify your answer." Proof 3 also matters for
**2025 Q7(a)**, because Śaṃkara's charge that unconscious nature cannot act purposively is answered
by showing the proof is from *power*, not from planning.

<a id="formal_session-074-upsc-traps-9508270d26"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** name proof 1 or proof 2 as the one-nature proof. They establish a root, not its unity.
- Do **not** render proof 3 as "from purposive activity". Write **from operation due to causal
  power**; the misreading turns a non-theistic system into a design argument.
- Do **not** answer 2019 with only the proof-name. The marks are in the bridge sentence.
- Do **not** confuse "one nature" with "one witness". Nature is one; witnesses are many.

<a id="formal_session-075-rapid-revision-b2fd00f1c3"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Five names in order: finitude · uniformity · power · cause–effect distinction · undivided source.
- Proof 5 ★ = the only one that proves **oneness**.
- *Sāṃkhyakārikā* 15 reads **śaktitaḥ pravṛtteś ca** — power, not planning.
- Bridge line: many roots would destroy the undividedness we reasoned from.

<a id="formal_session-076-session-7-the-three-qualities-equilibrium-and-modification-2ed2ea2644"></a>
### SESSION 7 — THE THREE QUALITIES, EQUILIBRIUM AND MODIFICATION

<a id="formal_session-077-definition-what-this-is-called-b35dcfedfc"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Nature is not a smooth blank; it is woven from three strands that are
always present together in different proportions. Change the proportion and you change the object,
the mood and the moral tone at once. Balance the three exactly and nothing manifests at all.

**Technical definition.** The three qualities (**guṇa**) are luminosity (**sattva**), activity
(**rajas**) and inertia (**tamas**). They are not three separable substances but three inseparable
constitutive tendencies within one nature. Their exact balance is the state of equilibrium
(**sāmyāvasthā**), in which nature remains unmanifest. Transformation within each quality's own
nature is homogeneous modification (**sarūpa-pariṇāma**); asymmetrical transformation producing
differentiated evolutes is heterogeneous modification (**virūpa-pariṇāma**).

<a id="formal_session-078-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-82f20ad178"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

A useful way in: the three qualities are simultaneously *ontological*, *psychological* and
*ethical*. The same luminosity that makes an object visible makes a mind clear and a temperament
serene. The same inertia that makes a stone heavy makes a mind dull and a temperament confused.
Sāṃkhya is claiming that one set of three factors explains physical properties, mental states and
moral tone together — an extremely economical claim, and a genuinely distinctive one.

The second thing to fix is the word "quality". These are not properties possessed by nature; they
are what nature is *made of*. Nature is not a thing that has three qualities; nature is the
equilibrium of the three.

<a id="formal_session-079-visual-the-three-strands-character-tone-function-and-state-c8feee5977"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE THREE STRANDS: CHARACTER, TONE, FUNCTION AND STATE

```text
  ┌──────────┬────────────────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
  │ QUALITY  │ CORE CHARACTER         │ AFFECTIVE    │ FUNCTION IN EVOLUTION        │
  │          │                        │ TONE         │                              │
  ├──────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ SATTVA   │ lightness,             │ pleasure     │ revelation, clarity,         │
  │          │ illumination, buoyancy │              │ manifestation, knowledge     │
  ├──────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ RAJAS    │ activity, restlessness,│ pain         │ energy, stimulation,         │
  │          │ propulsion             │              │ movement, mediation          │
  ├──────────┼────────────────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ TAMAS    │ heaviness, obstruction,│ delusion     │ concealment, resistance,     │
  │          │ inertia                │              │ fixation, material density   │
  └──────────┴────────────────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘

  TWO STATES OF THE SAME THREE STRANDS
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   EQUILIBRIUM (sāmyāvasthā)                DISTURBANCE
   the three in exact balance               asymmetrical relations begin
   nature = UNMANIFEST (avyakta)            nature = MANIFEST evolution
        │                                        │
        ▼                                        ▼
   SARŪPA-PARIṆĀMA                          VIRŪPA-PARIṆĀMA
   homogeneous modification:                heterogeneous modification:
   each quality transforms WITHIN            the qualities transform ACROSS
   its own nature                            one another
   ⇒ nature is dynamic even when             ⇒ actual cosmological evolution
     unmanifest; nothing differentiated        belongs here
     is yet produced

  ⚠ WHAT DISTURBS THE BALANCE: the mere PRESENCE of the witness (sannidhi) —
    functional activation in proximity, NOT a mechanical collision.
```

*What the diagram shows: one triad, two states, two kinds of modification. The 2021 question asks
for exactly the top table plus the bottom pair.*

<a id="formal_session-080-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-58e72648f3"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **The three characters.** Luminosity is clear and upward-moving; it makes cognition and
   manifestation possible. Activity is dynamic, agitating and projective; it supplies motion, effort
   and transition. Inertia is heavy, obscuring and resistant; it produces inertia, concealment and
   confusion.
2. **The affective mapping.** Pleasure is associated with luminosity, pain with activity and
   delusion with inertia. Write this explicitly — it is what makes the qualities psychological and
   not merely physical.
3. **Inseparability.** The qualities are not three separable substances but three inseparable
   constitutive tendencies within one nature, occurring together in every evolute in varying
   proportions. This is what lets a single triad explain unlimited qualitative variety.
4. **Equilibrium.** Before manifestation the three exist in equilibrium (**sāmyāvasthā**); in this
   state nature is unmanifest.
5. **Disturbance.** Manifest evolution begins when this equilibrium is disturbed in the presence of
   the witness. Disturbance should not be imagined as mechanical collision; Sāṃkhya speaks of
   functional activation in proximity.
6. **Homogeneous modification.** In equilibrium, each quality transforms within its own nature. This
   preserves the idea that nature is dynamic even in the unmanifest state without yet producing
   differentiated effects.
7. **Heterogeneous modification.** When equilibrium is disturbed, the qualities enter asymmetrical
   relations and produce differentiated evolutes. Actual cosmological evolution belongs here.

> **Why this matters beyond the 2021 question.** The mechanism of *all* Sāṃkhya causation is quality
> modification. When Session 13 says the effect is a real transformation of the cause, the
> transformation being spoken of is exactly this: the three qualities transforming and recombining
> to produce the differentiated principles.

<a id="formal_session-081-named-evidence-you-can-cite-78738a7f42"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- The three names with characters, affective tones and functions.
- **Equilibrium (sāmyāvasthā)** as the definition of the unmanifest state.
- **Homogeneous (sarūpa)** versus **heterogeneous (virūpa)** modification.
- The inseparability claim: three tendencies within one nature, not three substances.

<a id="formal_session-082-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-40498d6881"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The theory of the three qualities is Sāṃkhya's most economical achievement: a single triad is made
> to explain physical constitution, affective tone and moral temperament together, so that
> cosmology and psychology are not two subjects in this system but one.

<a id="formal_session-083-must-write-keywords-584880826f"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **luminosity (sattva) — pleasure — manifestation**
- **activity (rajas) — pain — motion**
- **inertia (tamas) — delusion — concealment**
- **equilibrium (sāmyāvasthā)**
- **homogeneous modification (sarūpa-pariṇāma)**
- **heterogeneous modification (virūpa-pariṇāma)**
- **modification of the qualities (guṇa-pariṇāma)**

<a id="formal_session-084-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-afc5ab1b51"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The three qualities are not properties of nature but its constituents, and their two
states of modification explain both why anything is manifest and why anything is unmanifest.

**Named evidence.** Luminosity, activity and inertia carry pleasure, pain and delusion respectively,
and function as manifestation, motion and concealment; their exact balance is equilibrium
(**sāmyāvasthā**), within which each quality still undergoes homogeneous modification, while
disturbance yields the heterogeneous modification that generates the manifest principles.

**Analysis.** The distinction between the two kinds of modification does real work: it lets Sāṃkhya
say that nature is never inert and never static, yet is genuinely unmanifest before evolution — so
dissolution is not annihilation and creation is not a beginning from nothing.

**Qualification.** What triggers the shift from one modification to the other remains the system's
most exposed point, since the witness is inactive and can only be present; Sāṃkhya therefore
describes the disturbance as functional activation in proximity rather than as an event with an
efficient cause.

<a id="formal_session-085-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-55870f27cb"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

**2021 Q6(b)** asks for the three qualities *and their modifications* — the second half is the
homogeneous/heterogeneous pair, which is where most answers lose marks. The triad also feeds
**2018 Q5(e)**, since differential distribution of the qualities is one of the arguments for a
plurality of witnesses.

<a id="formal_session-086-upsc-traps-ceb5fd2901"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** call the qualities three substances. They are inseparable constituent tendencies.
- Do **not** treat them as moral labels applied after the fact; they are constitutive.
- Do **not** stop at the table in a 2021-type question. The modifications carry half the marks.
- Do **not** say equilibrium means nature is inactive. It means nature is undifferentiated while
  still undergoing homogeneous modification.
- Do **not** describe the disturbance as a physical push from the witness.

<a id="formal_session-087-rapid-revision-64e7d39cf7"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Luminosity/pleasure/manifestation · activity/pain/motion · inertia/delusion/concealment.
- Together always, in varying proportion, in every evolute.
- Balance = unmanifest; disturbance = manifest.
- Homogeneous modification within a quality; heterogeneous modification across qualities.
- One line: *change the proportion and you change the world, the mind and the mood at once.*

<a id="formal_session-088-session-8-conscious-witness-nature-and-the-five-proofs-a43bb271e3"></a>
### SESSION 8 — CONSCIOUS WITNESS: NATURE AND THE FIVE PROOFS

<a id="formal_session-089-definition-what-this-is-called-5e4a4d720b"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** The witness is pure awareness. It does not think, act, change or
produce. It is not a soul that owns experiences the way a person owns property; it is the bare fact
that experience is *for* someone.

**Technical definition.** The conscious witness (**puruṣa**) is pure consciousness, self-luminous,
inactive, changeless and beyond the qualities. Consciousness is not a quality *in* the witness; it
is the witness's very essence. The witness is seer, witness and experiencer (**bhoktṛ**), but not
agent in the productive sense.

<a id="formal_session-090-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-ad2101598d"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

The easiest way to hold this is by subtraction. Take a human being and remove everything that
changes: the body, the senses, the coordinating mind, the ego that says "mine", the intellect that
decides. In most systems you have now removed the self as well. In Sāṃkhya you have removed
everything *except* the self, because all of those were nature.

What remains is not a thinner person. It is the condition under which any of that machinery could
appear to anyone at all. That is why the witness cannot be an object of perception: it is that for
which objects are.

<a id="formal_session-091-visual-the-five-proofs-as-one-cumulative-inference-e5cc862ea8"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE FIVE PROOFS AS ONE CUMULATIVE INFERENCE

```text
  THE SHARED PREMISE OF ALL FIVE
  everything in the objective complex is unconscious (jaḍa), instrumental, and
  PRESENTED to experience
                                    │
      ┌─────────────────┬───────────┴────────┬──────────────────┬────────────────────┐
      ▼                 ▼                    ▼                  ▼                    ▼
  1 saṅghāta-       2 triguṇādi-        3 adhiṣṭhānāt      4 bhoktṛ-           5 kaivalyārthaṃ
    parārthatvāt      viparyayāt                             bhāvāt              pravṛtteḥ
  ─────────────    ───────────────    ───────────────    ───────────────    ─────────────────
  body, senses,    the qualities      psychophysical     pleasure, pain     striving for
  mind, intellect  are objective,     life is            and delusion       release from
  are AGGREGATES   mutable, and       COORDINATED,       are EXPERIENCED    suffering is a
  (saṅghāta)       are the KNOWN      not disconnected                      central fact
      │                │              flashes                 │                  │
      ▼                ▼                  │                   ▼                  ▼
  an aggregate     the knower of      a conscious        objects/states     such striving is
  is instrumental  pleasure, pain     support in whose   require a          unintelligible
  ⇒ it exists FOR  and delusion       presence the       BHOKTṚ distinct    without a witness
    a user         cannot BE those    unconscious        from them          distinct from
    distinct       same changing      system becomes                        bondage-producing
    from it        states             an ORDER                              nature
      │                │                  │                   │                  │
      └────────────────┴──────────────────┴───────────────────┴──────────────────┘
                                          ▼
                    A NON-QUALITY-CONSTITUTED CONSCIOUS PRINCIPLE
                                    = PURUṢA
              inferential form of all five: SĀMĀNYATO-DṚṢṬA ANUMĀNA
```
