```text
              — from the general structure of objectifiable experience
                to a witness that is never itself an object

  ⚠ PROOF 3 IS NOT A GOD-PROOF. It infers a presiding WITNESS of lived order,
    not an intelligent maker of the world.
```

*What the diagram shows: five arguments with one premise and one conclusion. Presenting them as five
independent proofs weakens the answer; presenting them as a cumulative case is what "evaluate"
rewards.*

<a id="formal_session-092-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-c8a93f553b"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

**(1) Aggregates exist for another — saṅghāta-parārthatvāt.**
Body, senses, mind and intellect are aggregates or complexes (**saṅghāta**). Aggregates function
instrumentally, and an instrument is for a user or beneficiary distinct from it. Hence there must be
a conscious witness for whose experience and release the aggregate functions.

**(2) The witness must be other than the qualities — triguṇādi-viparyayāt.**
The qualities are objective, mutable and characterised by pleasure, pain and delusion. The knower of
pleasure, pain and delusion cannot be reducible to the same changing states that are known.
Therefore a non-quality-constituted conscious principle must be admitted.

**(3) Organised experience requires a presiding locus — adhiṣṭhānāt.**
Psychophysical life displays coordinated functioning rather than disconnected flashes. Sāṃkhya
infers a conscious support in whose presence the unconscious system becomes an experienced order.
**This is not a God-proof**; it is an inference to the witness as the presiding witness of lived
order. Marking this explicitly is worth marks, because it is the point at which a careless answer
accidentally imports theism into a non-theistic system.

**(4) Experience requires an enjoyer — bhoktṛ-bhāvāt.**
Pleasure, pain and delusion are experienced. Since these are presented as objects or states, there
must be an experiencer (**bhoktṛ**) distinct from them. Unconscious nature can *present* experience,
but it cannot be the subject *for whom* experience appears.

**(5) Striving for liberation presupposes a liberable witness — kaivalyārthaṃ pravṛtteḥ.**
Human striving for release from suffering is a central fact for Sāṃkhya. Such striving would be
unintelligible if there were no conscious principle distinct from bondage-producing nature. The
orientation toward isolation (**kaivalya**) therefore presupposes the witness.

**Evaluation — what "examine and evaluate" wants.**

- The proofs are **cumulative, not isolated**. Any one alone is answerable; together they argue that
  aggregates are instrumental, qualities are objectifiable, experience requires a witness, and
  liberation presupposes a self.
- Their epistemic form is uniform: general-correlation inference (**sāmānyato-dṛṣṭa anumāna**), from
  the general structure of objectifiable experience to a witness that is never itself an object.
- Their honest limit: they establish that experience has a *subject-pole*; they do not by themselves
  establish that the subject-pole is eternal, plural or inactive. Those come from further argument
  (Session 9) and from the system's soteriological requirements.

**The nature of the witness, tabulated.**

| Feature | Sāṃkhya position |
|---|---|
| Consciousness | essence, not quality |
| Activity | inactive, non-productive |
| Change | changeless, immutable |
| Relation to the qualities | entirely beyond them |
| Status | witness, seer, experiencer |

Two consequences follow immediately and must be written: the witness does not become bound in its
own nature — bondage belongs to misidentification; and the witness does not produce the world — all
production belongs to nature.

<a id="formal_session-093-named-evidence-you-can-cite-51be067091"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- All five Sanskrit proof-names with English glosses.
- **Aggregate (saṅghāta)**, **experiencer (bhoktṛ)**, **insentient (jaḍa)**.
- The explicit disclaimer that the third proof is not a God-proof.
- The uniform inferential form: general-correlation inference.

<a id="formal_session-094-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-b09bb3d9b4"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Sāṃkhya's proofs for the witness are best read as one argument in five movements: everything we
> can point to in the person is an instrument, and an instrument that is genuinely for no one is not
> an instrument at all.

<a id="formal_session-095-must-write-keywords-1d86d09357"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **saṅghāta-parārthatvāt** — aggregates exist for another
- **triguṇādi-viparyayāt** — other than the qualities
- **adhiṣṭhānāt** — presiding locus (not a God-proof)
- **bhoktṛ-bhāvāt** — experience requires an enjoyer
- **kaivalyārthaṃ pravṛtteḥ** — striving presupposes a liberable witness
- **self-luminous, inactive, changeless**
- **cumulative inference of the general-correlation kind**

<a id="formal_session-096-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-73d89299a0"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The five Sāṃkhya proofs for the conscious witness succeed as a cumulative case and fail
as five independent demonstrations.

**Named evidence.** They argue from the instrumentality of aggregates (**saṅghāta-parārthatvāt**),
from the objectivity and mutability of the qualities (**triguṇādi-viparyayāt**), from the
coordination of psychophysical life (**adhiṣṭhānāt**), from the presented character of pleasure,
pain and delusion (**bhoktṛ-bhāvāt**), and from the intelligibility of striving for release
(**kaivalyārthaṃ pravṛtteḥ**).

**Analysis.** Each proof taken alone invites a deflationary reply — a Buddhist can explain
coordination by causal series and ownership by convention — but the five together converge on a
single structural point: every candidate item inside the person is something *presented*, and a
domain of presentation without a subject-pole is not a domain of presentation at all.

**Qualification.** What the convergence yields is a witness, not yet an eternal, plural and wholly
inactive witness; those further properties are secured by the system's account of individuation and
liberation rather than by these inferences, and honest evaluation should say so.

<a id="formal_session-097-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-86692b22b3"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

**2022 Q5(a)** — "Examine and evaluate the proofs given by Sāṃkhya philosophy to prove the existence
of Puruṣa" — is answered exactly by the five proofs plus the cumulative-force evaluation and the
honest limit. The nature-table also underwrites **2019 Q5(d)** on the empirical self and the witness.

<a id="formal_session-098-upsc-traps-83e0b4db39"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** present the third proof as a proof of God. It infers a presiding *witness*.
- Do **not** say consciousness is a *property* of the witness; it is its essence.
- Do **not** call the witness an agent or a knower in the productive sense; it is the experiencer for
  whom cognition occurs.
- Do **not** stop at listing when the directive says "evaluate". Add cumulative force and limits.

<a id="formal_session-099-rapid-revision-d63245a092"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Five proofs: aggregates-for-another · other-than-qualities · presiding locus · enjoyer · striving.
- One premise (everything objective is unconscious and presented) and one form (general-correlation
  inference).
- Nature of the witness: essence-consciousness, inactive, changeless, beyond the qualities.
- Never bound in itself; never productive.

<a id="formal_session-100-session-9-plurality-of-witnesses-purposive-nature-and-empirical-self-ver-dec9018f66"></a>
### SESSION 9 — PLURALITY OF WITNESSES, PURPOSIVE NATURE, AND EMPIRICAL SELF VERSUS WITNESS

<a id="formal_session-101-definition-what-this-is-called-03808c7eb5"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Sāṃkhya holds that there are many witnesses, not one — even though
every witness is exactly alike. It also holds that unconscious nature behaves *for* those witnesses
without intending anything. And it holds that the everyday "I" is not a further entity but the
witness seen through the intellect.

**Technical definition.** Plurality of witnesses (**puruṣa-bahutva**) is numerical, not qualitative.
Nature acts for the sake of the witness (**puruṣārtha**) by supplying experience and finally the
possibility of discriminative release. The empirical self (**jīva**) is not a separate principle
(**tattva**) but the witness as empirically appearing through identification with intellect, I-maker,
mind, body and the karmic stream.

<a id="formal_session-102-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-cab7f7af57"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Three separate claims are packed together here, and separating them is most of the work.

- *How many witnesses?* Many — because your blindness is not mine and your release would not be
  mine.
- *Why does nature bother?* Because purposiveness, in this system, does not require intention; milk
  flows for the calf without deliberating.
- *What then is the everyday self?* Not a third thing between nature and witness, but the witness
  wearing the intellect.

If you write these as three separate answers, each with its own evidence, you will not blur them —
and blurring them is precisely what loses marks in the 2018 and 2019 questions.

<a id="formal_session-103-visual-the-plurality-argument-its-source-control-and-its-pressure-point-1a0a54dad4"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE PLURALITY ARGUMENT, ITS SOURCE CONTROL, AND ITS PRESSURE POINT

```text
  ARGUMENTS FOR MANY WITNESSES
  ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  ┌── FROM Sāṃkhyakārikā 18 (write these THREE first) ─────────────────────────┐
  │ 1 FIXED DISTRIBUTION of birth, death and sense-faculties                   │
  │   if there were ONE witness, one birth would be all births, one death all  │
  │   deaths, and all would be blind when one is blind                         │
  │ 2 NON-SIMULTANEITY OF ACTIVITY                                             │
  │   beings act at different times and in different ways; one self would make │
  │   all embodied streams act together                                        │
  │ 3 DIFFERENTIAL DISTRIBUTION OF THE THREE QUALITIES                         │
  │   beings differ as luminous, active or inert in temperament ⇒ distinct     │
  │   centres of experience                                                    │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
  ┌── SUPPLEMENTARY (add as REINFORCEMENT, not as a fourth Kārikā phrase) ─────┐
  │ 4 DIFFERENTIAL LIBERATION — if the self were one, release of one would be  │
  │   release of all; some remain bound while another may be released          │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  THE PRESSURE POINT (state it; do not hide it)
  ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  every witness is PURE, ATTRIBUTELESS, PARTLESS, ACTIONLESS consciousness
                                    │
                    what then makes them numerically MANY?
                                    │
        ┌───────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┐
        ▼                                                        ▼
  SĀṂKHYA ANSWER                                        ADVAITA REPLY
  plurality is INFERRED from the                        every difference cited is a
  non-transferability of experience,                    difference in LIMITING
  bondage and liberation                                ADJUNCTS (upādhi) — bodies,
        │                                               minds, quality-configurations
        ▼                                                        │
  the ultimate individuation of                                  ▼
  qualitatively identical witnesses                     ⇒ Sāṃkhya proves at most a
  remains the school's WEAKEST JOINT                      plurality of MENTAL STREAMS
  (say this — it is honest and it scores)                 (citta), not of witnesses
```

*What the diagram shows: three canonical arguments, one supplementary argument, and one admitted
weakness. Marking the fourth argument as supplementary is a source-control point the examiner can
check.*

<a id="formal_session-104-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-53fb399622"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

**A. Why witnesses are many.**

The first three arguments map directly onto *Sāṃkhyakārikā* 18: fixed distribution of birth, death
and faculties; non-simultaneous activity; and differential constitution by the qualities.
Differential liberation is a standard supplementary inference, **not** a fourth phrase in that
verse. In the exam, present the three canonical arguments first and add non-simultaneous liberation
only as reinforcement.

Plurality does not mean qualitative difference among witnesses; each witness is pure, inactive
consciousness, but they are numerically many.

**B. The presuppositions behind the plurality argument.** These are worth naming because "critically
discuss" directives reward them.

| Presupposition | Why it matters |
|---|---|
| Empirical individuation transfers upward | Differences in bodies and mental streams are taken to license an inference to distinct witnesses. |
| The witness is genuinely an experiencer (**bhoktṛ**) | If experience has no owner, the question "whose pleasure or pain?" loses its force. |
| Liberation is particular | Liberation is attributed to a particular witness's psychophysical stream, not to the collapse of a universal illusion. |
| Numerical plurality of attributeless consciousness is intelligible | The school accepts numerical difference without qualitative difference. |

**C. Objections and replies — the four that matter.**

- **Advaita / Śaṃkara.** Every difference cited is a difference in limiting adjuncts (**upādhi**) —
  bodies, minds, quality-configurations — not in consciousness itself. Pure consciousness, being
  without attribute, part, action or spatial location, admits no principle of individuation;
  therefore Sāṃkhya proves at most a plurality of mental streams (**citta**), not of witnesses.
  **Sāṃkhya's reply.** If there were one witness, the bondage of one would be the bondage of all and
  the release of one the release of all; the reflection model also requires distinct reflected loci
  in distinct intellects.
  **Residual force.** Advaita answers that bondage itself is adventitious and the many empirical
  selves are apparent, so the exchange terminates in a clash of first principles: Sāṃkhya trusts the
  plurality given in experience, while Advaita subordinates plurality to the unity given in
  scripture.
- **Nyāya.** Nyāya agrees that selves are many but objects to a purely inactive self; an agentless
  self cannot be the locus of moral desert, so Nyāya makes the self a substance with adventitious
  cognition, volition and effort.
  **Reply.** Agency belongs to nature's evolutes; ascribing agency to the witness is precisely the
  false appropriation (**abhimāna**) that bondage consists in.
- **Buddhist.** A permanent, changeless and actionless entity is causally idle and therefore, by the
  criterion of efficacy (**arthakriyā**), unreal.
  **Reply.** The witness's efficacy is not productive causation but mere presence (**sannidhi /
  sānnidhya**) that renders nature's activity experienced; without the witness the purposiveness of
  nature (**puruṣārtha**) is unintelligible.
- **Internal objection.** If witnesses are all identical in nature — pure, attributeless
  consciousness — what makes them numerically many?
  **Honest reply.** Plurality is inferred from the non-transferability of experience, bondage and
  liberation; but the ultimate individuation of qualitatively identical witnesses remains the
  school's weakest joint. Saying this is not a concession that costs marks; it is the mark of a
  candidate who has understood the problem.

**D. Purposive nature without intention.**

Nature acts for the sake of the witness (**puruṣārtha**) by providing experience and finally the
possibility of discriminative release. Four canonical images carry this:

| Image | What it explains |
|---|---|
| **Milk flowing for the calf** | unconscious functioning can still serve a purposive end |
| **The lame man and the blind man** | complementarity: the witness sees but cannot act; nature acts but cannot see |
| **The dancer who retires once she has been seen** | after discriminative knowledge nature no longer performs binding activity for that witness |
| **The modest woman who withdraws on being seen** | once her distinction from the witness is disclosed, concealment ends |

**Objection.** Unconscious teleology appears incoherent, because purposiveness seems to require
intelligence.
**Reply.** Purposive functioning need not be conscious planning; the quality-structure naturally
unfolds in the presence of the witness, just as milk nourishes the calf without deliberation.

**E. The empirical self is not a further principle.**

- The witness is the metaphysical reality: pure, inactive, changeless consciousness.
- The empirical self (**jīva**) is **not a separate principle**. It is the witness as empirically
  appearing through identification with intellect, I-maker, mind, body and the karmic stream.
- Therefore the empirical self is the conditioned everyday self, while the witness is the
  unconditioned conscious reality behind it.
- **Best formulation for the exam:** the empirical self is the witness as empirically conditioned
  through intellect; the witness is the metaphysical reality behind that appearance.

<a id="formal_session-105-named-evidence-you-can-cite-c5753bd888"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- ***Sāṃkhyakārikā* 18** for the three canonical plurality arguments, with differential liberation
  marked as supplementary.
- The four analogies: milk and calf, lame and blind, the dancer, the modest woman.
- Named objectors: Advaita on limiting adjuncts; Nyāya on moral desert; Buddhism on efficacy.
- **Limiting adjunct (upādhi)**, **mental stream (citta)**, **efficacy (arthakriyā)**, **mere
  presence (sannidhi)**.

<a id="formal_session-106-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-6677133a43"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Sāṃkhya defends a plurality of witnesses by an argument from non-transferability — my blindness is
> not yours and my release would not be yours — and the school's deepest embarrassment is that this
> excellent moral argument must individuate entities which, by its own account, have no features by
> which to differ.

<a id="formal_session-107-must-write-keywords-3699fbddfa"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- ***Sāṃkhyakārikā* 18**
- **numerical, not qualitative, plurality**
- **limiting adjunct (upādhi)** — the Advaita counter
- **efficacy (arthakriyā)** — the Buddhist counter
- **for the sake of the witness (puruṣārtha)**
- **milk-and-calf · lame-and-blind · the dancer · the modest woman**
- **the empirical self (jīva) is not a separate principle**

<a id="formal_session-108-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-309f24b501"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Sāṃkhya's plurality of witnesses is argued from moral and experiential
non-transferability, and the empirical self is a mode of appearance rather than an additional
entity.

**Named evidence.** *Sāṃkhyakārikā* 18 supplies fixed distribution of birth, death and faculties,
non-simultaneous activity and differential constitution by the qualities, with differential
liberation added as a supplementary inference; and the school explicitly denies that the empirical
self is a separate principle, treating it as the witness conditioned through intellect.

**Analysis.** These two theses are mutually supporting: because the empirical self is only the
witness seen through an intellect, differences among empirical selves can be read as evidence of
differences among witnesses, which is exactly the inference Advaita blocks by assigning every such
difference to limiting adjuncts.

**Qualification.** The Sāṃkhya position therefore stands or falls on whether numerically distinct
but qualitatively identical consciousnesses are intelligible; the school infers plurality from
non-transferable bondage and release, but concedes in effect that it cannot state the principle of
individuation itself.

<a id="formal_session-109-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-1bacf1d939"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

**2018 Q5(e)** — "Is Puruṣa one or many?" — is answered by section A plus the Advaita objection and
reply. **2019 Q5(d)** — the metaphysical status of the empirical self and the witness — is answered
by section E plus the reflection mechanism from Session 4. Section D is the load-bearing reply to
Śaṃkara's teleology charge in **2025 Q7(a)**.

<a id="formal_session-110-upsc-traps-785bc76f25"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** cite differential liberation as a fourth phrase of *Sāṃkhyakārikā* 18. It is
  supplementary.
- Do **not** say the witnesses differ in quality. The plurality is numerical only.
- Do **not** treat the empirical self as a twenty-sixth principle.
- Do **not** answer the Advaita objection by repeating the plurality arguments; answer it by the
  non-transferability of bondage and release and the requirement of distinct reflected loci.
- Do **not** claim nature "intends" anything. Purposiveness here is structural, not intentional.

<a id="formal_session-111-rapid-revision-d83ae6f9f3"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Three Kārikā-18 arguments + one supplementary; plurality is numerical only.
- Advaita: differences are in limiting adjuncts → at most many mental streams.
- Nyāya: an inactive self cannot own moral desert. Buddhist: no efficacy, so unreal.
- Nature works for the witness: milk-calf, lame-blind, dancer, modest woman.
- Empirical self = witness through intellect; not a separate principle.

<a id="formal_session-112-session-10-the-twenty-five-principles-the-complete-evolutionary-sequence-1508b32855"></a>
### SESSION 10 — THE TWENTY-FIVE PRINCIPLES: THE COMPLETE EVOLUTIONARY SEQUENCE

<a id="formal_session-113-definition-what-this-is-called-d2789f85d6"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** From one unmanifest nature the world unfolds in a fixed order:
intellect first, then the ego, then two branches — one producing the mind and the ten capacities,
the other producing the five subtle elements and from them the five gross elements. Counting nature
and the witness, the total is twenty-five.

**Technical definition.** The twenty-five principles (**tattva**) are: primordial material nature;
the great one or determinative intellect (**mahat / buddhi**); the I-maker (**ahaṃkāra**); the
coordinating mind (**manas**); five cognitive capacities (**jñānendriya**); five action capacities
(**karmendriya**); five subtle elements (**tanmātra**); five gross elements (**mahābhūta**); and the
conscious witness (**puruṣa**) as the twenty-fifth, which is not an evolute of nature.

<a id="formal_session-114-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-a91d98b5e4"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Three sentences will protect you through this entire session.

1. The great one and the determinative intellect are **the same principle** under two names — cosmic
   and individual.
2. The ego produces **two lines**, not three, because activity (**rajas**) supplies energy rather
   than a separate product-set.
3. The witness is number twenty-five but is **not an evolute**; it stands outside the productive
   series entirely.

Every recurring error in this topic is a violation of one of those three sentences.

<a id="formal_session-115-visual-the-evolution-tree-in-exact-order-75640b728f"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE EVOLUTION TREE IN EXACT ORDER

```text
                     PURUṢA  ── mere presence (sannidhi) ──┐
                 (25th principle, NOT an evolute)          │ disturbs
                                                           ▼
                              PRAKṚTI  ─ cause of all, effect of none
                                  │        (avyakta pradhāna, three qualities in balance)
                                  ▼
                      MAHAT / BUDDHI  ─ FIRST EVOLUTE
                      cosmic name = mahat · individual name = buddhi
                      ⚠ ONE principle, TWO names — never two stages
                                  │
                                  ▼
                          AHAṂKĀRA  ─ principle of individuation, the "I"-maker
                                  │
        ┌─────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┐
        │                         │                             │
  SĀTTVIKA LINE            RĀJASA FACTOR                 TĀMASA LINE
  (luminous aspect)        (active aspect)               (inert aspect)
        │                         │                             │
        ▼                  supplies ENERGY and             ▼
  MANAS  (mind)            operative force to BOTH   5 TANMĀTRA (subtle elements)
  5 JÑĀNENDRIYA            lines                     sound · touch · form ·
    hearing · touch ·      ⚠ produces NO separate    taste · smell potentials
    sight · taste · smell     set of principles                │
  5 KARMENDRIYA                                                 ▼
    speech · grasping ·                             5 MAHĀBHŪTA (gross elements)
    locomotion ·                                    ether · air · fire · water · earth
    excretion ·
    reproduction                     STANDARD CORRESPONDENCE
                                     sound → ether · touch → air · form → fire
                                     taste → water · smell → earth

  COUNT CHECK: 1 nature + 1 intellect + 1 ego + 1 mind + 5 + 5 + 5 + 5 = 24 evolutes
               + 1 witness = 25 principles
```

*What the diagram shows: one root, one first evolute, one individuator, then a two-line branching
with a third quality acting as energiser rather than producer.*

<a id="formal_session-116-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-5c641a8b7a"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

**The ordered list.**

| No. | Principle | Note |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **primordial nature (prakṛti)** | root material cause |
| 2 | **the great one / determinative intellect (mahat / buddhi)** | first evolute |
| 3 | **I-maker (ahaṃkāra)** | principle of individuation |
| 4 | **coordinating mind (manas)** | internal coordinator |
| 5–9 | **five cognitive capacities (jñānendriya)** | cognitive senses |
| 10–14 | **five action capacities (karmendriya)** | action faculties |
| 15–19 | **five subtle elements (tanmātra)** | subtle elements |
| 20–24 | **five gross elements (mahābhūta)** | gross elements |
| 25 | **conscious witness (puruṣa)** | pure consciousness, non-evolute |

**The complete instrument and element taxonomy.**

| Family | English-first members | Function / evolution |
|---|---|---|
| Five cognitive capacities | hearing (**śrotra**), touch (**tvak**), sight (**cakṣus**), taste (**rasanā**), smell (**ghrāṇa**) | disclose sound, touch, form or colour, taste and smell |
| Five action capacities | speech (**vāk**), grasping (**pāṇi**), locomotion (**pāda**), excretion (**pāyu**), reproduction (**upastha**) | perform the five principal outward functions |
| Five subtle elements | sound-potential (**śabda**), touch-potential (**sparśa**), form-potential (**rūpa**), taste-potential (**rasa**), smell-potential (**gandha**) | arise from the inert side of the I-maker with active-quality energy |
| Five gross elements | ether (**ākāśa**), air (**vāyu**), fire or light (**tejas**), water (**ap**), earth (**pṛthivī**) | arise from the subtle-element series and compose the gross world |

The standard correspondence runs sound → ether, touch → air, form → fire, taste → water and smell
→ earth. Do **not** import Advaita's later fivefold grossification (**pañcīkaraṇa**) into a basic
classical Sāṃkhya evolution answer.

**The fourfold cause–effect classification — the fastest control on the whole list.**

| Status | Principles | Count |
|---|---|---:|
| Cause but not effect (evolvent only) | primordial nature alone | 1 |
| Both cause and effect | intellect, I-maker and the five subtle elements | 7 |
| Effect only | mind, five cognitive capacities, five action capacities, five gross elements | 16 |
| Neither cause nor effect | the conscious witness | 1 |

This classification is the quickest way to control the full sequence and to prevent miscounting the
cosmic and determinative intellect as two separate principles.

**Cosmic intellect, determinative intellect and the I-maker — the 2024 second half.**

- The great one (**mahat**) and the determinative intellect (**buddhi**) are the **same principle**.
  The great one is the cosmic aspect or name; the determinative intellect is the individual or
  psychological aspect or name.
- The I-maker (**ahaṃkāra**) emerges **from** that principle as the principle of individuation or
  I-sense.
- Therefore the sequence is nature → the great one / determinative intellect → the I-maker.
- **This is a classic trap.** Never write the great one and the determinative intellect as two
  separate stages.

**What each of the three does.**

- The determinative intellect determines, judges, ascertains and discriminates; it is the locus
  where reflected consciousness is most conspicuous in empirical life.
- The I-maker appropriates functions into the form "I know", "I act", "I experience"; false selfhood
  crystallises here, which is why it is central to bondage.
- The mind coordinates. In one line: *mind coordinates, I-maker appropriates, intellect determines.*

**The three lines.**

- From the luminous aspect of the I-maker arise the mind, the five cognitive capacities and the five
  action capacities. This line accounts for the cognitive and conative equipment of empirical life.
- From the inert aspect of the I-maker arise the five subtle elements, and from those the five gross
  elements. This line accounts for subtle-to-gross material manifestation.
- The active quality supplies activity, energy and operative force to **both** lines. The
  active-aspect I-maker does **not** produce a separate set of principles. Write this explicitly; it
  is repeatedly confused.

**Why the sequence matters philosophically.** The sequence shows that cosmology and psychology are
internally linked in Sāṃkhya: the same nature that yields matter also yields the faculties through
which matter is experienced. That is the philosophical point a 20-mark answer must reach after
narrating the list.

<a id="formal_session-117-named-evidence-you-can-cite-30b8c5f96b"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- The ordered twenty-five with their numbering.
- The fourfold classification with the counts 1 / 7 / 16 / 1.
- The named members of all four five-fold families.
- The subtle-to-gross correspondence, and the explicit exclusion of fivefold grossification.

<a id="formal_session-118-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-7fd6f97244"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The list of twenty-five principles is not an inventory but an argument: by deriving intellect,
> ego, mind and the senses from the same root as the elements, Sāṃkhya makes the knower's equipment
> as much a part of nature as the known world, and therefore makes liberation a matter of
> discrimination rather than of escape.

<a id="formal_session-119-must-write-keywords-0c310d4667"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **twenty-five principles (tattva); twenty-four evolutes plus the witness**
- **the great one / determinative intellect (mahat / buddhi) — one principle, two names**
- **I-maker (ahaṃkāra) as the principle of individuation**
- **luminous line · inert line · active quality as energiser only**
- **subtle elements (tanmātra) → gross elements (mahābhūta)**
- **fourfold cause–effect classification: 1 / 7 / 16 / 1**

<a id="formal_session-120-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-6635bb26be"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** The Sāṃkhya evolutionary sequence is ordered by a single principle — subtler and more
internal before grosser and more external — and that ordering is what carries its philosophical
point.

**Named evidence.** Nature yields the great one or determinative intellect, which yields the I-maker,
from whose luminous aspect arise the mind and the ten capacities and from whose inert aspect arise
the five subtle elements and then the five gross elements, with the active quality supplying energy
to both lines and producing no separate set; the fourfold classification fixes this as one evolvent,
seven both, sixteen effects only, and one neither.

**Analysis.** Because determination precedes individuation and individuation precedes both the
sensory apparatus and the elements, Sāṃkhya locates the "I" inside nature rather than at its origin —
which is exactly what allows bondage to be described as a misplacement within the series and
liberation as its correction.

**Qualification.** The order is a doctrinal derivation, not an observational chronology; the school
does not claim that intellect existed before elements in datable time, but that the manifest world
is intelligible only if the more determinate is derived from the less determinate.

<a id="formal_session-121-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-c24cdd9bdd"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

**2024 Q7(a) (20 marks, 10+10)** asks for the evolution of nature as propounded in the
*Sāṃkhyakārikā* and, in that context, the difference between the determinative intellect, the great
one and the I-maker. The first ten marks are the tree; the second ten are the one-principle-two-names
point plus the three functional roles.

<a id="formal_session-122-upsc-traps-d16cd26284"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** write the great one and the determinative intellect as two separate stages.
- Do **not** give the active-quality aspect its own product-set. It energises both lines.
- Do **not** derive the mind from the inert line, or the subtle elements from the luminous line.
- Do **not** count the witness among the evolutes; it is the twenty-fifth principle but a
  non-evolute.
- Do **not** insert Advaita's fivefold grossification into the classical sequence.
- Do **not** reverse the elemental correspondence: smell goes with earth, sound with ether.

<a id="formal_session-123-rapid-revision-4d966a2815"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Nature → intellect → ego → (luminous: mind + 5 + 5) and (inert: 5 subtle → 5 gross); activity
  energises both.
- 24 evolutes + witness = 25.
- 1 evolvent-only · 7 both · 16 effects-only · 1 neither.
- Cosmic name and individual name of one principle; the ego individuates.
- One line: *the equipment of knowing is itself a product of the known.*

<a id="formal_session-124-session-11-subtle-body-transmigration-and-karma-03b19839f0"></a>
### SESSION 11 — SUBTLE BODY, TRANSMIGRATION AND KARMA

<a id="formal_session-125-definition-what-this-is-called-c445bf8057"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Something has to travel between lives, or rebirth is unintelligible.
It cannot be the witness, because the witness never moves. So Sāṃkhya gives the job to a subtle
psychophysical vehicle that carries dispositions from one gross body to the next.

**Technical definition.** The subtle body (**liṅga-śarīra / sūkṣma-śarīra**) is the continuing
psychophysical vehicle between gross embodiments. It is commonly counted as eighteen constituents:
the determinative intellect, the I-maker, the eleven sense and action capacities including the mind,
and the five subtle elements. It is conditioned by dispositions (**bhāva**) and by the eight broad
tendencies of the intellect: merit and demerit, knowledge and ignorance, detachment and
non-detachment, power and powerlessness.

<a id="formal_session-126-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-aa34dcc70e"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Picture a seal and a series of wax blocks. The seal is not the wax; it does not become the wax; but
it stamps its pattern on each new block in turn. Sāṃkhya's subtle body is like the seal: a stable
carrier of dispositions that receives a new gross body at each embodiment.

Now note what is *not* in the picture. The witness is not the seal and not the wax. It does not
migrate at all. This is the point most answers get wrong, and it is exactly what the school's
strictest formulations insist on.

<a id="formal_session-127-visual-what-migrates-what-does-not-and-what-carries-the-pattern-86bbbbf575"></a>
#### VISUAL — WHAT MIGRATES, WHAT DOES NOT, AND WHAT CARRIES THE PATTERN

```text
  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  DOES NOT MIGRATE                                                          │
  │  PURUṢA — never acts, never moves, is never really bound or liberated      │
  │  ⇒ transmigration is NOT the journey of a soul in this system              │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  MIGRATES: THE SUBTLE BODY (liṅga-śarīra / sūkṣma-śarīra)                  │
  │  commonly counted as EIGHTEEN constituents                                 │
  │  ┌──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────┬─────────────────────┐  │
  │  │ intellect  1 │ I-maker    1 │ mind + 10        │ five subtle         │  │
  │  │              │              │ capacities    11 │ elements          5 │  │
  │  └──────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────┴─────────────────────┘  │
  │              1 + 1 + 11 + 5 = 18                                           │
  │                                                                            │
  │  CONDITIONED BY the EIGHT BROAD TENDENCIES OF INTELLECT (bhāva)            │
  │    merit ⟷ demerit · knowledge ⟷ ignorance                                 │
  │    detachment ⟷ non-detachment · power ⟷ powerlessness                     │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  IS DISCARDED: THE GROSS BODY                                              │
  │  produced from parental material; falls away at death                      │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  THE PRESSURE POINT
  moral ownership must be explained through the STABLE ASSOCIATION of one
  subtle stream with ONE numerically distinct witness — an association the
  system asserts but does not further ground.
```

*What the diagram shows: a three-tier separation — what never moves, what carries the pattern, and
what is discarded — plus the ethical problem the separation creates.*

<a id="formal_session-128-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-e4bbd7cfd8"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

1. **The vehicle.** The subtle body is the continuing psychophysical vehicle between gross
   embodiments. It is not a ghost or a soul; it is a functional aggregate drawn from the evolutes
   already enumerated in Session 10.
2. **The count.** Eighteen constituents: determinative intellect, I-maker, eleven capacities
   including the mind, and five subtle elements. Note that the eleven includes the mind — this is why
   the count is eighteen and not seventeen.
3. **The dispositions.** The subtle body is described as primordially arisen within the evolutionary
   cycle and conditioned by dispositions and the eight broad tendencies of the intellect: merit and
   demerit, knowledge and ignorance, detachment and non-detachment, power and powerlessness. These
   eight are the moral-cognitive profile that the vehicle carries.
4. **Death.** At death the gross body produced from parental material falls away, while the subtle
   body carries dispositions and karmic tendencies into another embodiment.
5. **The strict formulation about the witness.** The pure conscious witness neither acts nor
   literally migrates. Transmigration belongs to the subtle body and the empirical self-complex
   operating *for* a particular witness.
6. **The pressure point — state it.** This preserves the inactivity of the witness, but it creates a
   difficulty: moral ownership must be explained through the stable association of a subtle stream
   with one numerically distinct witness. Sāṃkhya asserts that association; it does not further
   ground it. This is the same weak joint identified in Session 9, now seen from the ethical side.

<a id="formal_session-129-named-evidence-you-can-cite-a3f6749f32"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **Subtle body (liṅga-śarīra / sūkṣma-śarīra)** with its eighteen-constituent count.
- The **eight tendencies of the intellect** as four contrasted pairs.
- The strict claim that the witness neither acts nor literally migrates.
- The contrast with the gross body produced from parental material.

<a id="formal_session-130-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-cdc107b9fd"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Sāṃkhya buys the inactivity of consciousness at the price of relocating rebirth: what travels
> between lives is not a self but a disposition-bearing subtle body, so the school must then explain
> how a purely passive witness comes to own a moral career it never conducted.

<a id="formal_session-131-must-write-keywords-d6a3464dee"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **subtle body (liṅga-śarīra / sūkṣma-śarīra)**
- **eighteen constituents (1 + 1 + 11 + 5)**
- **dispositions (bhāva)**
- **eight tendencies: merit/demerit, knowledge/ignorance, detachment/non-detachment,
  power/powerlessness**
- **the gross body from parental material**
- **the witness neither acts nor migrates**

<a id="formal_session-132-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-445775fe2d"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Sāṃkhya explains rebirth without a transmigrating self by assigning continuity to a
subtle body rather than to the witness.

**Named evidence.** The subtle body is counted as eighteen constituents — determinative intellect,
I-maker, the eleven capacities including the mind, and the five subtle elements — and is conditioned
by dispositions and the intellect's eight tendencies of merit and demerit, knowledge and ignorance,
detachment and non-detachment, power and powerlessness; at death the gross body from parental
material falls away while this vehicle continues.

**Analysis.** The manoeuvre is internally consistent, because everything that changes has already
been placed inside nature, so the carrier of karmic tendency can be constructed entirely from
evolutes without compromising the changelessness of the witness.

**Qualification.** Its cost is ethical rather than cosmological: since the witness never acts, moral
desert has to be secured by the stable association of one subtle stream with one witness, and that
association is asserted by the system rather than derived from anything more basic.

<a id="formal_session-133-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-cc8b514651"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

Supports **2019 Q5(d)** on the empirical self and the witness, because the empirical self is exactly
the witness plus this subtle-body complex. It is also the correct reply to any Buddhist or Nyāya
challenge in **2018 Q5(e)** that plural inactive witnesses cannot ground moral responsibility.

<a id="formal_session-134-upsc-traps-154520edb8"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** say the witness transmigrates. Say the subtle body does.
- Do **not** confuse the subtle body with the five subtle elements; the elements are five of its
  eighteen constituents.
- Do **not** omit the mind when counting the eleven capacities; the total is eighteen.
- Do **not** treat the eight tendencies as ethical maxims. They are dispositional states of the
  intellect.

<a id="formal_session-135-rapid-revision-79a8d6d94a"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Subtle body = 1 intellect + 1 ego + 11 capacities + 5 subtle elements = 18.
- Eight tendencies in four contrasted pairs.
- Gross body from parental material is discarded; subtle body carries dispositions.
- The witness never acts and never moves.
- One line: *the pattern travels; the light does not.*

<a id="formal_session-136-session-12-pre-existence-of-the-effect-the-five-arguments-bb9d581d6e"></a>
### SESSION 12 — PRE-EXISTENCE OF THE EFFECT: THE FIVE ARGUMENTS

<a id="formal_session-137-definition-what-this-is-called-2ecf901f1e"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Sāṃkhya says the effect is already in its material cause before it is
produced — not visibly, but as a determinate capacity. Production does not create being; it makes
latent being patent.

**Technical definition.** The doctrine of the pre-existence of the effect in the cause
(**satkāryavāda**) holds that the effect pre-exists in its material cause, so that production is
manifestation (**abhivyakti**), not creation out of sheer non-being. Sāṃkhya's specific form is the
doctrine of real transformation (**pariṇāmavāda**).

<a id="formal_session-138-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-b00edf2b07"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Read the thesis carefully, because the careless version is indefensible. Sāṃkhya does **not** say a
miniature gross tree is hidden inside the mango seed. It says that this seed, and not sand, has the
determinate capacity for this tree — and that having such a determinate capacity is what "the effect
pre-exists" means.

If you write the careless version, the examiner's rival positions demolish it in one line. If you
write the careful version — *latent effect, material continuity, real transformation* — the doctrine
becomes formidable.

<a id="formal_session-139-visual-five-arguments-each-with-its-counter-and-reply-f37878672c"></a>
#### VISUAL — FIVE ARGUMENTS, EACH WITH ITS COUNTER AND REPLY

```text
  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ 1  asadakaraṇāt — a NON-ENTITY CANNOT BE PRODUCED                             │
  │    no operation yields a hare's horn or oil from sand                         │
  │    ◄ Nyāya: you equivocate — we deny only that the POT exists AS A POT before  │
  │      production, not that anything exists                                     │
  │    ► reply: the "not-yet" effect must still be SOMETHING in the material       │
  │      cause, else the operation has no determinate terminus                    │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2  upādāna-grahaṇāt — a DETERMINATE MATERIAL CAUSE IS TAKEN UP                 │
  │    for curd take milk, not water; for cloth take threads, not clay             │
  │    ◄ counter: the restriction is explained by causal CAPACITY, not by the      │
  │      effect's pre-existence                                                   │
  │    ► reply: capacity in the required DETERMINATE sense just IS the latent      │
  │      presence of that effect in that material continuum                       │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 3  sarvasambhavābhāvāt — EVERYTHING CANNOT ARISE FROM EVERYTHING               │
  │    non-existence as such is INDISTINGUISHABLE, so it cannot explain why        │
  │    curd comes from milk and not from water                                    │
  │    ◄ counter: restrictions come from the constitution of causes                │
  │    ► reply: a constitution capable of producing only X IS the latent presence  │
  │      of X as a determinate effect-potential                                   │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 4  śaktasya śakya-karaṇāt — THE POTENT PRODUCES ONLY THE PRODUCIBLE            │
  │    milk has potency for curd, not for cloth; threads for cloth, not curd       │
  │    ◄ Nyāya: potency is a RELATIONAL PROPERTY of the cause, not the effect      │
  │      in latent form                                                           │
  │    ► reply: a merely relational potency cannot explain DETERMINATE production  │
  │      unless the producible form is already grounded in the material cause     │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 5  kāraṇa-bhāvāt — THE EFFECT HAS THE BEING OF THE CAUSE                       │
  │    cloth is threads in an arrangement: same material basis, weight and         │
  │    quality-continuity; ornament and gold differ in form and name only          │
  │    ◄ Nyāya: the WHOLE (avayavin) is a NEW substance with new properties —      │
  │      a cloth covers, separate threads do not                                  │
  │    ► reply: the new properties are new ARRANGEMENTS (saṃsthāna), not new       │
  │      substances created out of non-being                                      │
  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

*What the diagram shows: this is not a list of five assertions but five miniature debates. Reproducing
the counter and the reply is what converts a description into an argument.*

<a id="formal_session-140-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-38cf27382d"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

**(1) Because what does not exist cannot be brought into being — asadakaraṇāt.**
A sheer non-entity cannot be produced. No amount of causal operation can produce a hare's horn or
oil from sand; production presupposes something determinate to be produced. A potter's effort can
manifest a pot from clay, but it cannot manifest a square circle from nothing.
*Nyāya objection:* Sāṃkhya equivocates between the absolutely non-existent and the merely
not-yet-existent; Nyāya denies only that the pot exists *as a pot* before production.
*Reply:* the not-yet-existent effect must still be something in the material cause, otherwise the
operation would have no determinate terminus.

**(2) Because a determinate material cause is taken up — upādāna-grahaṇāt.**
Specific effects require specific material causes. One who wants curd takes milk, not water; one who
wants cloth takes threads, not clay. The selection of mango seed rather than sand for a mango tree
shows a determinate causal base.
*Objection:* the restriction may be explained by causal capacity rather than by the pre-existence of
the effect.
*Reply:* causal capacity in the required determinate sense just is the latent presence of that
effect in that material continuum.

**(3) Because everything cannot arise from everything — sarvasambhavābhāvāt.**
If effects were wholly non-existent before production, any cause could produce any effect.
Non-existence as such is indistinguishable; therefore it cannot explain why curd arises from milk
and not from water. Cloth comes from threads, not from milk; curd comes from milk, not from threads.
*Objection:* restrictions come from the constitution of causes, not from effects already existing in
causes.
*Reply:* a constitution capable of producing only one effect is precisely the latent presence of
that effect as a determinate effect-potential.

**(4) Because the efficient can produce only what it is capable of producing — śaktasya
śakya-karaṇāt.**
A cause has a determinate potency (**śakti**) directed toward a determinate effect. A potency for
something is unintelligible unless that something is in some manner already there as the object of
the potency. Milk has the potency for curd, not for cloth; threads have the potency for cloth, not
for curd.
*Nyāya objection:* potency is a relational property of the cause, not the effect in latent form.
*Reply:* a merely relational potency cannot explain determinate production unless the producible
form is already grounded in the material cause.

**(5) Because the effect has the nature or being of the cause — kāraṇa-bhāvāt.**
The effect is non-different from the cause in substance. Cloth is nothing over and above threads in
a certain arrangement; it has the same material basis, weight and quality-continuity. Ornament and
gold differ in form and name, but the material substance remains gold.
*Nyāya objection:* the whole (**avayavin**) is a new substance with new properties — a cloth covers,
but separate threads do not.
*Reply:* the new properties are new arrangements or configurations (**saṃsthāna**), not new
substances created out of non-being.

> **The formulation to memorise for the mango-seed question.** Write it as *latent effect + material
> continuity + real transformation*. Do **not** write it as a miniature gross mango tree hidden
> inside the seed.

<a id="formal_session-141-named-evidence-you-can-cite-87c5c84ad5"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- All five Sanskrit argument-names with English glosses.
- The standard examples: hare's horn, oil from sand, milk and curd, threads and cloth, gold and
  ornament, mango seed and mango tree.
- **Whole (avayavin)** and **arrangement (saṃsthāna)** — the Nyāya objection and the Sāṃkhya reply.
- The prohibition on the "miniature tree in the seed" reading.

<a id="formal_session-142-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-fad0d0c9ff"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The doctrine of the pre-existent effect is best defended as an argument about determinacy: since
> sheer non-being is featureless and therefore cannot explain why *this* cause yields *that* effect,
> the restriction we actually observe in production must already be written into the material cause.

<a id="formal_session-143-must-write-keywords-fdebfe4d98"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **satkāryavāda**; production as manifestation (**abhivyakti**)
- **asadakaraṇāt · upādāna-grahaṇāt · sarvasambhavābhāvāt · śaktasya śakya-karaṇāt ·
  kāraṇa-bhāvāt**
- **determinate potency (śakti)**
- **whole (avayavin)** versus **arrangement (saṃsthāna)**
- **latent effect + material continuity + real transformation**

<a id="formal_session-144-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-2a10de26f2"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Sāṃkhya's case for the pre-existent effect rests on the observed determinacy of
production rather than on any claim that effects are visible inside their causes.

**Named evidence.** The five arguments run from the impossibility of producing a non-entity, from
the taking up of a determinate material cause, from the fact that everything does not arise from
everything, from potency being directed at a determinate producible, and from the effect's sharing
the being of the cause — with milk and curd, threads and cloth, and gold and ornament as the
standing examples.

**Analysis.** The third argument carries the greatest weight, because non-existence is featureless
and a featureless antecedent cannot discriminate outcomes; hence any theory that begins from sheer
prior absence must smuggle the required determinacy back in through the constitution of the cause,
which is what Sāṃkhya means by latent presence.

**Qualification.** The Nyāya rejoinder is not thereby refuted: an opponent may locate determinacy in
a relational potency rather than in a latent effect, and the debate then turns on whether a relation
without a determinate relatum can do explanatory work — a question the two schools answer by
different first principles.

<a id="formal_session-145-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-5464631941"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

The core of **2020 Q7(a) (20 marks)** on the mango tree and the mango seed, where the demand is
explicitly to explain the process "by rejecting their rival perspectives". It is also the
Sāṃkhya-side content required by the routed **2021 Q5(a)** ("Does the seed contain the tree?") and
**2023 Q8(b)** on prior non-existence.

<a id="formal_session-146-upsc-traps-6f442dc01e"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** say the gross effect is already manifest, or that a small tree sits inside the seed.
- Do **not** conflate the doctrine of the pre-existent effect with Sāṃkhya's specific form of it;
  Advaita is also a pre-existence theorist.
- Do **not** omit the Nyāya counters. "Rejecting rival perspectives" is part of the printed demand.
- Do **not** treat argument 5 as a claim of identity; the effect differs from the cause in state and
  arrangement.

<a id="formal_session-147-rapid-revision-cbc4dfaf3a"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Five names: non-entity · determinate material · not-everything-from-everything · potency ·
  being-of-the-cause.
- Examples: hare's horn, sand and oil, milk and curd, threads and cloth, gold and ornament.
- Nyāya counters: equivocation, capacity, constitution, relational potency, the new whole.
- Reply pattern: determinacy must be grounded in the material cause.
- One line: *featureless non-being cannot explain a selective world.*

<a id="formal_session-148-session-13-real-transformation-against-the-rival-causal-theories-2ac6cd1224"></a>
### SESSION 13 — REAL TRANSFORMATION AGAINST THE RIVAL CAUSAL THEORIES

<a id="formal_session-149-definition-what-this-is-called-7eced8a60d"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Sāṃkhya's version of the pre-existent effect is that the cause really
turns into the effect. Advaita's version is that the cause only appears to. Nyāya denies the
pre-existence altogether. Buddhism denies the enduring cause. Four positions, one question.

**Technical definition.** The doctrine of real transformation (**pariṇāmavāda**) holds that the
effect is a genuine modification of its material cause, effected through the modification of the
qualities (**guṇa-pariṇāma**). It is contrasted with the doctrine of apparent transformation
(**vivartavāda**), with the doctrine of the non-pre-existent effect (**asatkāryavāda**), and with
dependent origination (**pratītyasamutpāda**).

<a id="formal_session-150-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-d81167a7c7"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Hold four sentences apart:

- **Sāṃkhya:** the clay really becomes the pot.
- **Advaita:** the rope only appears to be a snake; nothing really becomes anything.
- **Nyāya:** the pot did not exist before, and production is a genuinely new beginning.
- **Buddhism:** there is no enduring clay at all; there is only conditioned arising.

The examiner's favourite trap is the first pair, because Sāṃkhya and Advaita agree that being does
not come from non-being — and then disagree about everything else. Name the agreement, then make the
disagreement decisive.

<a id="formal_session-151-visual-the-four-way-causal-map-95fa1fe03e"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE FOUR-WAY CAUSAL MAP

```text
                     DOES THE EFFECT PRE-EXIST IN ITS MATERIAL CAUSE?
                                        │
                ┌───────────────────────┴────────────────────────┐
              YES — satkārya orientation                        NO
                │                                                │
    ┌───────────┴────────────┐                        ┌──────────┴───────────┐
    ▼                        ▼                        ▼                      ▼
 SĀṂKHYA                  ADVAITA                  NYĀYA-VAIŚEṢIKA        BUDDHISM
 pariṇāmavāda             vivartavāda              asatkāryavāda /        pratītyasamutpāda
                                                   ārambhavāda
 ─────────────────        ─────────────────        ─────────────────      ─────────────────
 transformation is        transformation is        effect ABSENT as       no enduring
 REAL                     APPARENT                 effect beforehand      material root at all
                                                   (prāgabhāva)
 cause = PRAKṚTI          cause = BRAHMAN          production = a NEW     becoming through
                                                   BEGINNING             DEPENDENT ARISING
 world = REAL evolute     world = ultimately       effect = a FRESH
                          appearance               product
 mechanism = guṇa-        mechanism = super-       model = continuity     model = conditioned
 pariṇāma                 imposition               BROKEN at production   series without an
                                                                          enduring substratum

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ THE EXAM-CRITICAL AGREEMENT                                                  │
  │ Sāṃkhya AND Advaita both deny sheer creation from non-being — so both can be │
  │ placed under a broad satkārya orientation. THE DECISIVE DIFFERENCE IS        │
  │ PARIṆĀMA versus VIVARTA: real transformation yields dualistic realism;       │
  │ apparent transformation yields non-dual idealism.                            │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

*What the diagram shows: one question, one binary split, and then a second split inside the "yes"
branch which is where most of the marks lie.*

<a id="formal_session-152-core-upsc-the-argument-in-full-bcc2b5cf16"></a>
#### CORE UPSC — THE ARGUMENT IN FULL

**What real transformation asserts.** Sāṃkhya's pre-existence doctrine is specifically the doctrine
of real transformation: the effect is a real transformation of the cause. It is **not** apparent
transformation, because the world is not a mere appearance superimposed on an unchanged cause; it is
the real evolution of nature. The mechanism is the modification of the qualities: the three qualities
transform and recombine to produce the differentiated principles. In equilibrium each quality
transforms homogeneously within its own nature; when equilibrium is disturbed the qualities transform
heterogeneously and generate the manifest evolutes.

**Three standing objections and their replies.**

| Objection | Sāṃkhya reply |
|---|---|
| If the effect already exists, causal effort becomes pointless. | Production is manifestation (**abhivyakti**) of what is latent; effort removes obstruction and supplies conditions — it does not create being out of non-being. |
| The doctrine abolishes the distinction between cause and effect. | Cause and effect differ by **state (avasthā)**: the cause is latent or unmanifest, the effect patent or manifest. |
| The doctrine implies the world was always exactly as it is now. | The gross effect is *not* already manifest; only the determinate capacity or effect-form exists latently in the cause. |

**Contrast with apparent transformation (Advaita).**

| Issue | Sāṃkhya | Advaita Vedānta |
|---|---|---|
| Type of transformation | real | apparent |
| Technical term | **pariṇāma** | **vivarta** |
| Cause | primordial nature | Brahman |
| Status of the world | real evolute | ultimately appearance |

Both may be placed under a broad pre-existence orientation, but their accounts of transformation
differ radically. This is the exact point on which the 2018 fifteen-marker turns.

**How the Advaitins react to Sāṃkhya's real transformation of nature, and how Sāṃkhya defends
itself.** The Advaita reaction has three parts: an unconscious first principle cannot be the
scripturally described conscious cause; a real transformation of the ultimate would make the ultimate
mutable and hence dependent; and the world's dependence is better explained by superimposition than
by evolution. Sāṃkhya's defence also has three parts: the continuity we observe between determinate
causes and determinate effects is real and requires a real material continuity; treating the world as
appearance leaves the *selectivity* of production unexplained, since appearances too would then be
arbitrary; and Sāṃkhya's ultimate is not made mutable by evolution, because nature is *by definition*
the principle whose being is modification, unlike a changeless Brahman.

**Contrast with the non-pre-existent effect (Nyāya-Vaiśeṣika).**

| Issue | Sāṃkhya | Nyāya-Vaiśeṣika |
|---|---|---|
| Effect before production | pre-existent in the cause | absent as effect — prior non-existence (**prāgabhāva**) |
| Production | manifestation | new beginning |
| Causal model | continuity through transformation | the effect as a fresh product |

This contrast is directly relevant to the routed 2023 question on prior non-existence and Sāṃkhya
causation, and to the routed 2021 question "Does the seed contain the tree?".

**Contrast with dependent origination (Buddhism).** Buddhism explains becoming through dependent
origination rather than an enduring unmanifest material root; Sāṃkhya explains becoming through the
transformation of enduring nature. A crisp contrast line is usually enough unless the question
explicitly asks for a Buddhist comparison.

<a id="formal_session-153-named-evidence-you-can-cite-815df82bb8"></a>
#### NAMED EVIDENCE YOU CAN CITE

- **pariṇāma** versus **vivarta** versus **prāgabhāva / ārambhavāda** versus
  **pratītyasamutpāda**.
- **Manifestation (abhivyakti)** and **difference of state (avasthā)** as the two standard replies.
- Quality-modification as the mechanism, with the homogeneous/heterogeneous pair from Session 7.

<a id="formal_session-154-answer-grabbing-line-write-adapt-in-the-exam-96d0868d5a"></a>
#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The dispute between Sāṃkhya and Advaita is not about whether being can come from non-being — both
> deny that — but about whether an ultimate principle can change without ceasing to be ultimate, and
> Sāṃkhya's answer is that it can, because its ultimate is defined as that whose very being is
> modification.

<a id="formal_session-155-must-write-keywords-14a3c35bde"></a>
#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **real transformation (pariṇāmavāda)** versus **apparent transformation (vivartavāda)**
- **non-pre-existence (asatkāryavāda) / new beginning (ārambhavāda)**
- **prior non-existence (prāgabhāva)**
- **manifestation (abhivyakti)**; **difference of state (avasthā)**
- **modification of the qualities (guṇa-pariṇāma)**
- **dependent origination (pratītyasamutpāda)** as the Buddhist contrast

<a id="formal_session-156-how-to-write-the-paragraph-claim-named-evidence-analysis-qualification-471861745b"></a>
#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — CLAIM -> NAMED EVIDENCE -> ANALYSIS -> QUALIFICATION

**Claim.** Sāṃkhya's distinctive causal thesis is not the pre-existence of the effect, which it
shares with Advaita, but the reality of the transformation by which the effect is manifested.

**Named evidence.** Its mechanism is the modification of the qualities, homogeneous within each
quality during equilibrium and heterogeneous when equilibrium is disturbed; against the charge of
redundant effort it answers that production is manifestation (**abhivyakti**), and against the charge
that cause and effect collapse it answers that they differ by state (**avasthā**).

**Analysis.** This lets Sāṃkhya occupy a position no rival holds: unlike Nyāya it keeps material
continuity unbroken through production, and unlike Advaita it keeps the resulting world fully real,
so that bondage and liberation are events in a real order rather than corrections of an illusion.

**Qualification.** The position is exposed on exactly one flank: an ultimate that genuinely changes
invites the Vedāntic question of what sustains its changes, and Sāṃkhya's answer — that nature is by
nature modification — is a definition rather than a further explanation.

<a id="formal_session-157-exam-application-where-this-has-been-asked-1fef07639c"></a>
#### EXAM APPLICATION — WHERE THIS HAS BEEN ASKED

**2018 Q7(b) (15 marks)** asks precisely how the Advaita Vedāntins react to Sāṃkhya's real
transformation of nature and how the Sāṃkhyas defend their position. **2020 Q7(a)** requires the
rival-rejection half of this session. The routed **2022 Q5(e)** on whether apparent transformation is
the logical development of real transformation is answered from the same map.

<a id="formal_session-158-upsc-traps-eff151e1e2"></a>
#### UPSC TRAPS

- Do **not** claim the pre-existence doctrine is unique to Sāṃkhya. Advaita holds it too.
- Do **not** describe Advaita as denying causation. It denies *real* transformation.
- Do **not** say Nyāya denies that the material cause is determinate; it denies that the effect
  pre-exists *as* the effect.
- Do **not** answer the 2018 question with the five pre-existence arguments alone; the demand is the
  Advaita reaction *and* the Sāṃkhya defence.

<a id="formal_session-159-rapid-revision-9afe15cf0c"></a>
#### RAPID REVISION

- Four positions: real transformation · apparent transformation · new beginning · dependent arising.
- Shared premise with Advaita: no being from non-being. Decisive difference: real versus apparent.
- Three replies: manifestation · difference of state · only the capacity is latent.
- Nyāya contrast term: prior non-existence.
- One line: *the effect is old in substance and new in state.*

<a id="formal_session-160-session-14-bondage-discriminative-knowledge-isolation-and-non-theism-9f0c242167"></a>
### SESSION 14 — BONDAGE, DISCRIMINATIVE KNOWLEDGE, ISOLATION AND NON-THEISM

<a id="formal_session-161-definition-what-this-is-called-5a5f10c2f5"></a>
#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition.** Bondage is a mistake, not a chain. Liberation is the correction of the
mistake, not an escape or a reward. And no God is required at either end.

**Technical definition.** Bondage is non-discrimination (**aviveka**) between the witness and nature.
Liberation is discriminative knowledge (**viveka-jñāna**) that "I am not nature", and the resulting
state is isolation (**kaivalya**) — the aloneness of the witness. Liberation while the body persists
is **jīvanmukti**; final cessation of embodiment is **videhamukti**. The classical account is
non-theistic (**nirīśvara**).

<a id="formal_session-162-simple-start-the-gateway-idea-74f5cb3f38"></a>
#### SIMPLE START — THE GATEWAY IDEA

Two words to keep separate: *isolation* and *union*. Almost every other Indian soteriology moves the
self toward something — identity with Brahman, service to God, cessation of a series. Sāṃkhya moves
the self away from something. Isolation is disentanglement, not merger and not annihilation.

The second thing to fix: nothing actually changes in the witness at liberation. What changes is that
nature stops performing for it. That is why the dancer image is the most precise picture the school
has.

<a id="formal_session-163-visual-the-bondage-to-isolation-circuit-fdc3b61484"></a>
#### VISUAL — THE BONDAGE-TO-ISOLATION CIRCUIT

```text
  BONDAGE (aviveka)                                LIBERATION (viveka-jñāna)
  ═════════════════════════════════════════        ═══════════════════════════════════════
  intellect reflects consciousness                 the distinction between witness-
        │                                          consciousness and quality-evolutes
        ▼                                          becomes STEADY
  FALSE IDENTIFICATION                                     │
        │                                                  ▼
        ▼                                          FALSE IDENTIFICATION ENDS
  the self APPEARS as agent, enjoyer,                      │
  sufferer and knower in the empirical                     ▼
  sense                                            KAIVALYA — the aloneness /
        │                                          isolation of the witness
        ▼                                          ⚠ NOT merger · NOT annihilation ·
  ⚠ bondage is NOT a real alteration IN              NOT union with God
    the witness; it is mistaken
    identification through reflected
    consciousness

  THE FOUR CANONICAL IMAGES                    JĪVANMUKTI → VIDEHAMUKTI
  ──────────────────────────────────────       ──────────────────────────────────────
  lame + blind   complementarity of a          POTTER'S WHEEL: the wheel keeps
                 seer who cannot act and       turning after the impulse ceases
                 an actor who cannot see       ⇒ the body continues by PRIOR MOMENTUM
  dancer         she retires once she has        after discriminative knowledge
                 been seen                     ⇒ videhamukti = final cessation of
  modest woman   concealment ends on             embodiment when residual momentum ends
                 being seen
  milk for calf  unconscious functioning       STRICT FORMULATION
                 can still serve an end        in strict Sāṃkhya the witness is NEVER
                                               really bound, liberated or transmigrating;
                                               these belong to nature and the subtle body,
                                               and knowledge ends FALSE ATTRIBUTION

  WHY NO GOD IS NEEDED (nirīśvara)  — five standard arguments

  2 an eternally fulfilled God has NO MOTIVE to create a painful world
  3 if God distributes results under karma, divine independence is limited;
    if distribution is arbitrary, justice fails
  4 an immutable conscious God cannot be the MATERIAL source of a changing
    unconscious world under Sāṃkhya's cause-effect continuity
```
